using Lemma
5.61
and the proof is finished.
We should consider the three following cases:

\begin{array}[]{cccc}&(1.1)&(1.2)&(1.3)\end{array}

In the cases (1.1), (1.2) and (1.3) we have respectively (see the proof of Lemma
4.34
)

\displaystyle\Delta(Y_{1},Y_{2},Y_{3})\sim\Delta(Y_{1},Y_{2}),\quad\Delta(Y_{2},Y_{3},Y_{1})\sim\Delta(Y_{2},Y_{1}),

(4.61)

\displaystyle\Delta(Y_{1},Y_{2},Y_{3})\sim\Delta(Y_{1},Y_{3}),\quad\Delta(Y_{3},Y_{1},Y_{2})\sim\Delta(Y_{3},Y_{1}),

(4.62)

\displaystyle\Delta(Y_{1},Y_{2},Y_{3}),\quad\Delta(Y_{2},Y_{3},Y_{1}),\quad\Delta(Y_{3},Y_{1},Y_{2}).

(4.63)

By (
4.48
) and Lemma
4.32

we have respectively in the cases (1.1)–(1.3):

\displaystyle\nu_{12}(C_{1},C_{2}):=\|C_{1}Y_{1}+C_{2}Y_{2}\|^{2}\sim\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}b_{1n}\Big(C_{1}a_{1n}+C_{2}a_{2n}\Big)^{2},

(4.64)

\displaystyle\nu_{13}(C_{1},C_{3}):=\|C_{1}Y_{1}+C_{3}Y_{3}\|^{2}\sim\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}b_{1n}\Big(C_{1}a_{1n}+C_{3}a_{3n}\Big)^{2},

(4.65)

\displaystyle\nu(C_{1},C_{2},C_{3})\!=\!\|C_{1}Y_{1}+\!C_{2}Y_{2}\!+\!C_{3}Y_{3}\|^{2}\!\sim\!

\displaystyle\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}b_{1n}\Big(C_{1}a_{1n}\!+\!C_{2}a_{2n}\!+\!C_{3}a_{3n}\Big)^{2}.

(4.66)

Remark 4.4
.

We have three cases (1.1.1), (1.2.1) and (1.3.1) according to whether respectively the expressions in
(
4.64
), (
4.65
) or (
4.66
) are
divergent. We can approximate in these cases respectively
D_{1n}
and
D_{2n}
in (
4.61
),
D_{1n}
and
D_{3n}
in (
4.62
) all
D_{1n},\,D_{2n},\,D_{3n}
in (
4.63
). The proof of irreducibilty is finished in these cases
because we have
D_{rn},\,x_{2n},\,x_{3n}\,\eta\,\,{\mathfrak{A}}^{3}
for some
1\leq r\leq 3
.

If the opposite holds,
we have three different cases:

\displaystyle(1.1.0)\quad\|C_{1}Y_{1}+C_{2}Y_{2}\|<\infty\quad\text{for some}\quad(C_{1},C_{2})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\setminus\{0\},

\displaystyle(1.2.0)\quad\|C_{1}Y_{1}+C_{3}Y_{3}\|<\infty\quad\text{for some}\quad(C_{1},C_{3})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\setminus\{0\},

\displaystyle(1.3.0)\quad\nu(C_{1},C_{2},C_{3})<\infty\quad\text{for some}\quad(C_{1},C_{2},C_{3})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}\setminus\{0\}.

Recall that by (
2.44
) we have

S^{L}_{1,23}(\mu,t,s)=\sum_{n\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\left[\frac{t^{2}}{4}\frac{b_{1n}}{b_{2n}}+\frac{s^{2}}{4}\frac{b_{1n}}{b_{3n}}+\frac{b_{1n}}{2}\big(\!-\!2a_{1n}\!+\!ta_{2n}\!+\!sa_{3n}\big)^{2}\right].

Remark 4.5
.

In the case (1.1.0) we have
\Sigma^{12}=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{b_{1n}}{b_{2n}}=\infty
, since

S^{L}_{1,23}(\mu,t,0)=\infty,\quad\text{ but}\quad\nu_{12}(C_{1},C_{2})<\infty,

and
\Sigma^{13}=\infty
, since

S^{L}_{13}(\mu)=\sum_{n\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\frac{b_{1n}}{2}\Big(\frac{1}{2b_{3n}}+a_{3n}^{2}\Big)=\infty,\quad\text{but}\quad\|Y_{3}\|^{2}\sim\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}b_{1n}a_{3n}^{2}<\infty
, see (
2.43
) for definition of
S^{L}_{kr}(\mu)
.

In the case (1.2.0) we conclude that
\Sigma^{13}=\infty
, since
S^{L}_{1,23}(\mu,0,s)=\infty
, but
\nu_{13}(C_{1},C_{3})<\infty
, and
\Sigma^{12}=\infty
, since
S^{L}_{12}(\mu)=\sum_{n\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\frac{b_{1n}}{2}\Big(\frac{1}{2b_{2n}}+a_{2n}^{2}\Big)=\infty
, but
\|Y_{2}\|^{2}\sim\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}b_{1n}a_{2n}^{2}<\infty
.

In the case (1.3.0) we have
\Sigma^{12}=\Sigma^{13}=\infty
, since
S_{1,23}^{L}(\mu,t,s)=\infty,
but
\nu(C_{1},C_{2},C_{3})\sim\!\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}b_{1n}\Big(C_{1}a_{1n}\!+\!C_{2}a_{2n}\!+\!C_{3}a_{3n}\Big)^{2}<\infty
.

So, it remains to consider only the three following cases, when
\Sigma^{12}\!=\!\Sigma^{13}\!=\!\infty
:

\begin{array}[]{cccc}&(1.1.0)&(1.2.0)&(1.3.0)\end{array}

4.4.5
Approximation of
D_{2n}
and
D_{3n}
, 2

Recall that by Lemma
5.64
we have
D_{3n}\,\eta\,\,{\mathfrak{A}}^{3}\Leftrightarrow\Sigma_{3}(\mu)=\infty
where
\Sigma_{3}(\mu)
is defined by (
5.21
)

\Sigma_{3}(\mu):=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{\frac{1}{2b_{3k}}}{C_{k}+a_{1k}^{2}+a_{2k}^{2}+a_{3k}^{2}},\quad C_{k}=\frac{1}{2b_{1k}}+\frac{1}{2b_{2k}}+\frac{1}{2b_{3k}}.

Similarly, by analogue of Lemma
5.64
we can prove that

D_{2n}\,\eta\,\,{\mathfrak{A}}^{3}\Leftrightarrow\Sigma_{2}(\mu)=\infty
, where
\Sigma_{2}(\mu)
is defined as follows

\Sigma_{2}(\mu):=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{\frac{1}{2b_{2k}}}{C_{k}+a_{1k}^{2}+a_{2k}^{2}+a_{3k}^{2}}.

If one of
\Sigma_{2}(\mu)
or
\Sigma_{3}(\mu)
is infinite, we can approximate
D_{2n}
or
D_{3n}
and the proof is finished.
If
\Sigma_{2}(\mu)+\Sigma_{3}(\mu)<\infty
, we conclude that

\displaystyle\Sigma_{23}(\mu):=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{\frac{1}{2b_{2k}}+\frac{1}{2b_{3k}}}{C_{k}+a_{1k}^{2}+a_{2k}^{2}+a_{3k}^{2}}<\infty.

(4.67)

4.4.6
Approximation of
D_{2n}
and
D_{3n}
, 3

By Lemmas
5.62
–
5.63
we have

\displaystyle D_{2l}{\bf 1}\in\langle x_{2k}A_{kl}{\bf 1}\mid k\in{\mathbb{Z}}\rangle\quad\Leftrightarrow\quad\Delta(Y_{22},Y_{23},Y_{21})=\infty,

\displaystyle D_{3l}{\bf 1}\in\langle x_{3k}A_{kl}{\bf 1}\mid k\in{\mathbb{Z}}\rangle\quad\Leftrightarrow\quad\Delta(Y_{33},Y_{31},Y_{32})=\infty,

where vectors
Y_{rs}
for
2\leq r\leq 3,\,\,1\leq s\leq 3
are defined by (
5.17
)–(
5.20
).
We can not prove that
\Delta(Y_{22},Y_{23},Y_{21})=\infty
or
\Delta(Y_{33},Y_{31},Y_{32})=\infty
.
We can try to approximate
D_{3n}
using Lemma
5.65
or
to approximate
D_{2n}
using an analogue of Lemma
5.65
, but it does not work.
Therefore, to approximate
D_{3n}
we are forced to prove Lemma
5.66
the refinement of Lemma
5.65
and its analogue for
D_{2n}
, see Remark
4.6
below.

4.4.7
Two technical lemmas

Lemma 4.38
.

Let
a_{1},a_{2}\not\in l_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
and
C_{1}a_{1}+C_{2}a_{2}\in l_{2}
for some
(C_{1},C_{2})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\setminus\{0\},\,\,C_{2}\not=0
, where
a_{r}=(a_{rk})_{k\in\mathbb{Z}},\,\,1\leq r\leq 2
.
Then we have

\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{a^{2}_{1k}}{1+a_{2k}^{2}}=\infty.

(4.68)

Proof 17
.

We set
Y_{r}=a_{r}
, in the case (1.1.0) when
C_{1}Y_{1}+C_{2}Y_{2}=h\in l_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
with
C_{1}C_{2}>0

(we have
C_{1}C_{2}\!\not=\!0
)
we should take
a_{2}=-a_{1}+h
, in the case when
C_{1}C_{2}<0
we take
a_{2}=a_{1}+h
. The series
\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{a^{2}_{1k}}{1+a_{2k}^{2}}
will remain equivalent with the initial one, if we replace

(C_{1},C_{2})
with
(\pm 1,1)
in the expression for
h
.
Fix a small
\varepsilon\!>\!0
and a large
N\!\in\!\mathbb{N}
.
Since
|\pm a+b|\leq|a|+|b|
,
we get

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{a^{2}_{1k}}{1+a_{2k}^{2}}=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{a^{2}_{1k}}{1+\big(\pm a_{1k}+h_{k}\big)^{2}}\geq\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{a^{2}_{1k}}{1+a^{2}_{1k}+2|a_{1k}||h_{k}|+h^{2}_{k}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{two-sim-ser}}}{{\sim}}

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{a^{2}_{1k}}{1+2|a_{1k}||h_{k}|+h^{2}_{k}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(*)}}{{>}}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}}\frac{a^{2}_{1k}}{1+2|a_{1k}|\varepsilon+\varepsilon^{2}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{two-ser-alpha}}}{{\sim}}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}}a^{2}_{1k}=\infty,

where
\mathbb{Z}_{N}:=\{n\in\mathbb{Z}\mid|n|>N\}
. The inequality (*) holds,
since
h\in l_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
and we have
\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}}h^{2}_{k}<\varepsilon^{2}
for sufficiently large
N\in\mathbb{N}
.
∎

Lemma 4.39
.

Let
a_{1},a_{2},a_{3}\not\in l_{2}(\mathbb{Z})

and

C_{1}a_{1}+C_{2}a_{2}+C_{3}a_{3}\in l_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
for some
(C_{1},C_{2},C_{3})\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\setminus\{0\},\,\,C_{3}\not=0
, where

a_{r}=(a_{rk})_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}
for
1\leq r\leq 3
.
Then we have

\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{a^{2}_{1k}+a^{2}_{2k}}{1+a_{3k}^{2}}=\infty.

(4.69)

Proof 18
.

We set
Y_{r}=a_{r}
,
in the case (1.3.0), we have
C_{1}a_{1}+C_{2}a_{2}+C_{3}a_{3}=h\in l_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
for some
(C_{1},C_{2},C_{3})\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
, see Remark
4.4
. We can take
C_{3}=1
, then
a_{3}=-C_{1}a_{1}-C_{2}a_{2}+h
.
When
C_{1}=0
or
C_{2}=0
lemma is reduced to Lemma
4.38
.
Suppose
C_{1}C_{2}\not=0
.
The series
\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{a^{2}_{1k}+a^{2}_{2k}}{1+a_{3k}^{2}}
will remain equivalent with the initial one, if we replace

(C_{1},C_{2},C_{3})
with
(\pm 1,\pm 1,1)
in the expression for
h
.
Fix a small
\varepsilon>0
and a large
N\in\mathbb{N}
. Suppose the opposite, i.e.,

\displaystyle\infty\!>\!\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{a^{2}_{1k}+a^{2}_{2k}}{1+\big(\pm a_{1k}\pm a_{2k}+h_{k}\big)^{2}},\,\,\,\text{then}\,\,\,\infty\!>\!\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{(|a_{1k}|+|a_{2k}|)^{2}}{1+\big(\pm a_{1k}\pm a_{2k}+h_{k}\big)^{2}}

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{(|a_{1k}|+|a_{2k}|)^{2}}{1+a^{2}_{1k}+a^{2}_{2k}+2|a_{1k}||a_{2k}|+2|a_{1k}||h_{k}|+2|a_{2k}||h_{k}|+h^{2}_{k}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{two-sim-ser}}}{{\sim}}

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{(|a_{1k}|+|a_{2k}|)^{2}}{1+2|a_{1k}||h_{k}|+2|a_{2k}||h_{k}|+h^{2}_{k}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(*)}}{{>}}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}}\frac{(|a_{1k}|+|a_{2k}|)^{2}}{1+2\big(|a_{1k}|+|a_{2k}|\big)\varepsilon+\varepsilon^{2}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{two-ser-alpha}}}{{\sim}}

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}}(|a_{1k}|+|a_{2k}|)^{2}=\infty,

where
\mathbb{Z}_{N}:=\{n\in\mathbb{Z}\mid|n|>N\}
, contradiction. The inequality (*) holds,
since
h\in l_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
and we have
\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}}h^{2}_{k}<\varepsilon^{2}

for sufficiently large
N\in\mathbb{N}
.
∎

Remark 4.6
.

It is possible to prove an
analogue of
Lemma
5.66
to approximate
D_{2n}
with corresponding expressions
\Sigma_{2}(D,s),\,\Sigma^{\vee}_{2}(D,s)
and
\Sigma_{3}(D),\,\Sigma^{\vee}_{3}(D)
.
If one of the expressions
\Sigma_{2}(D,s),\,\Sigma^{\vee}_{2}(D,s),\,\Sigma_{3}(D,s)
or
\Sigma^{\vee}_{3}(D,s)
is divergent for some sequence
s=(s_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}
, we can approximate
D_{2k}
or
D_{3k}
and the proof is finished when
S=(0.1.1)
in the cases (a) and (b).
Suppose that for all sequence
s=(s_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}
we have

\Sigma_{2}(D,s)+\Sigma^{\vee}_{2}(D,s)+\Sigma_{3}(D,s)+\Sigma^{\vee}_{3}(D,s)<\infty.

Then, in particularly, we have for
s^{(r)}=(s_{rk})_{k\in\mathbb{Z}},\,\,2\leq r\leq 3

with
\frac{s^{2}_{rk}}{b_{rk}}\equiv 1

\displaystyle\infty>\Sigma_{2}(D,s^{(2)})+\Sigma^{\vee}_{2}(D,s^{(2)})+\Sigma_{3}(D,s^{(3)})+\Sigma^{\vee}_{3}(D,s^{(3)})\sim

\displaystyle\Sigma_{2}(D)+\Sigma^{\vee}_{2}(D)+\Sigma_{3}(D)+\Sigma^{\vee}_{3}(D)=

(4.70)

\displaystyle\sum_{k}\frac{\frac{1}{2b_{2k}}+a^{2}_{2k}+\frac{1}{2b_{3k}}+a^{2}_{3k}}{C_{k}+a^{2}_{1k}+a^{2}_{2k}+a^{2}_{3k}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{two-sim-ser}}}{{\sim}}\sum_{k}\frac{\frac{1}{2b_{2k}}+a^{2}_{2k}+\frac{1}{2b_{3k}}+a^{2}_{3k}}{\frac{1}{2b_{1k}}+a^{2}_{1k}}=:\Sigma^{\vee}_{23}(D)

\displaystyle\sim\sum_{k}\frac{\frac{b_{1k}}{b_{2k}}+2b_{1k}a^{2}_{2k}+\frac{b_{1k}}{b_{3k}}+2b_{1k}a^{2}_{3k}}{1+2b_{1k}a^{2}_{1k}}\!\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{alpha}}}{{\sim}}\!\sum_{k}\frac{a^{2}_{2k}+a^{2}_{3k}}{1+a^{2}_{1k}}=:\Sigma^{a}_{23}(D).

Remark 4.7
.

In the case (1.1.0) (resp. the case (1.2.0)) we have
\|Y_{3}\|^{2}\sim\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}a_{3n}^{2}<\infty

(resp.
\|Y_{2}\|^{2}\sim\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}a_{2n}^{2}<\infty
) and therefore,

\Sigma^{a}_{23}(D)\sim\sum_{k}\frac{a^{2}_{2k}}{1+a^{2}_{1k}}=\infty,\quad\text{resp.}\quad\Sigma^{a}_{23}(D)\sim\sum_{k}\frac{a^{2}_{3k}}{1+a^{2}_{1k}}=\infty,

by Lemma
4.38
, contradicting (
4.70
).
In the case (1.3.0) we have four cases:

(0) when
C_{1}C_{2}C_{3}\not=0
,
C_{1}a_{1}+C_{2}a_{2}+C_{3}a_{3}=h\in l_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
,

(1) when
C_{1}=0
hence,
C_{2}C_{3}\not=0
,
C_{2}a_{2}+C_{3}a_{3}=h\in l_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
,

(2) when
C_{2}=0
hence,
C_{1}C_{3}\not=0
,
C_{1}a_{1}+C_{3}a_{3}=h\in l_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
,

(3) when
C_{3}=0
hence,
C_{1}C_{2}\not=0

C_{1}a_{1}+C_{2}a_{2}=h\in l_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
.

In the case (0) we have
\Sigma^{a}_{23}(D)=\infty
by Lemma
4.39
,
contradicting (
4.70
). In the cases (2) and (3) we get
\Sigma^{a}_{23}(D)=\infty
by Lemma
4.38
, contradicting (
4.70
).
Therefore, one of the expressions
\Sigma_{2}(D,s),\,\Sigma^{\vee}_{2}(D,s),\,

\Sigma_{3}(D,s)
or
\Sigma^{\vee}_{3}(D,s)
is convergent hence, we can approximate
D_{2n}
or
D_{3n}
and the proof is finished.
To study the case (1) we need the following statement.

Lemma 4.40
.

Let
C_{2}Y_{2}+C_{3}Y_{3}=h_{23}\in l_{2}
for some
(C_{2},C_{3})\in\big(\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\}\big)^{2}

and
C_{1}Y_{1}\!+\!C_{2}Y_{2}\not\in l_{2}
or
C_{1}Y_{1}\!+\!C_{3}Y_{3}\not\in l_{2}
for all
(C_{1},C_{r})\in\big(\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\}\big)^{2}
, then

\Delta(Y_{1},Y_{2},Y_{3})=\infty.

(4.71)

Proof 19
.

To prove (
4.71
)
we have by (
8.15
)

\displaystyle\Delta(Y_{1},Y_{2},Y_{3})=\frac{\Gamma(Y_{1})+\Gamma(Y_{1},Y_{2})+\Gamma(Y_{1},Y_{3})+\Gamma(Y_{1},Y_{2},Y_{3})}{1+\Gamma(Y_{2})+\Gamma(Y_{3})+\Gamma(Y_{2},Y_{3})}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(*)}}{{>}}

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma(Y_{1},Y_{2})\!+\!\Gamma(Y_{1},Y_{3})}{1+(1+c_{2})\Gamma(Y_{2})+\Gamma(Y_{3})}\!\sim\!\frac{\Gamma(Y_{1},Y_{2})+\Gamma(Y_{1},Y_{3})}{\Gamma(Y_{2})+\Gamma(Y_{3})}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{sim(2,3)}}}{{\sim}}

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma(Y_{1},Y_{2})+\Gamma(Y_{1},Y_{3})}{2\Gamma(Y_{2})}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{sim(2,3)}}}{{\sim}}\frac{\Gamma(Y_{1},Y_{2})}{\Gamma(Y_{2})}+\frac{\Gamma(Y_{1},Y_{3})}{\Gamma(Y_{3})}=\infty,

(4.72)

\displaystyle\Gamma(Y_{2})\!\sim\!\Gamma(Y_{3}),\quad\text{since}\quad C_{2}Y_{2}+C_{3}Y_{3}=h\in l_{2}.

(4.73)

The relation (*) holds by the inequality

\Gamma(Y_{2},Y_{3})\leq c_{2}\Gamma(Y_{2})
, since
C_{2}Y_{2}\!+\!C_{3}Y_{3}\!\in\!l_{2}
for some
(C_{1},C_{3})\in\big(\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\}\big)^{2}
, the relation (
4.72
) holds by Lemma
8.83
.
To prove (
4.73
) we get since
Y_{2}\not\in l_{2}
and
h\in l_{2}
,

\frac{\Gamma(Y_{3})}{\Gamma(Y_{2})}=\frac{\|Y_{3}\|^{2}}{\|Y_{2}\|^{2}}=\frac{\|Y_{2}+h\|^{2}}{\|Y_{2}\|^{2}}\leq\Big(\frac{\|Y_{2}\|+\|h\|}{\|Y_{2}\|}\Big)^{2}=1,

If
C_{1}Y_{1}\!+\!C_{2}Y_{2}\not\in l_{2}
for all
(C_{1},C_{2})\in\big(\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\}\big)^{2}
, or
C_{1}Y_{1}\!+\!C_{3}Y_{3}\not\in l_{2}
for all
(C_{1},C_{3})\in\big(\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\}\big)^{2}
, by Lemma
4.40
we get
\Delta(Y_{1},Y_{2},Y_{3})=\infty
hence, we can approximate
D_{1n}
using Lemma
5.61
and the proof is finished.
If
C_{1}Y_{1}\!+\!C_{2}Y_{2}=h_{12}\in l_{2}
for some for
(C_{1},C_{2})\in\big(\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\}\big)^{2}
or

C_{1}Y_{1}\!+\!C_{3}Y_{3}=h_{13}\in l_{2}
for some
(C_{1},C_{3})\in\big(\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\}\big)^{2}
,
then
we have
h_{12}+\alpha h_{23}=C_{1}Y_{1}\!+\!C_{2}Y_{2}+\!C_{3}Y_{3}\in l_{2}
or
h_{12}+\beta h_{13}=C_{1}Y_{1}\!+\!C_{2}Y_{2}+\!C_{3}Y_{3}\in l_{2}

with
C_{1}C_{2}C_{3}\not=0
for an appropriate
\alpha\beta\not=0
, and we are in the case (0).
∎

4.4.8
Case (c)

In this case both
\rho_{2}(C_{2},-C_{3})
and
\rho_{3}(C_{2},-C_{3})
are finite, i.e.,
we are in the case (2)
therefore, we can not approximate
x_{2n}x_{2t},\,x_{3n}x_{3t}
by Lemmas
5.53
–
5.54
. By Lemma
4.36

\Sigma_{23}(s)<\infty

and hence,

\Sigma_{23}(C_{2},C_{3})=\infty
.
Indeed, reasoning as in Remark
2.5

we see that

\mu^{L_{\tau_{23}(\phi,s)}}\perp\mu,\,\,\,\phi\in[0,2\pi),\,\,s>0\Leftrightarrow\Sigma_{23}(s)+\Sigma_{23}(C_{2},C_{3})\!=\!\infty,\,\,s>0,

(4.74)

for
(C_{2},C_{3})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\setminus\{0\}
.
where
\tau_{23}(\phi,s),\,\,\Sigma_{23}(s)
and
\Sigma_{23}(C_{2},C_{3})

are defined as follows:

\displaystyle\tau_{23}(\phi,s)=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}1&0&0\\
0&\cos\phi&s^{2}\sin\phi\\
0&s^{-2}\sin\phi&-\cos\phi\end{array}\right),

\displaystyle\Sigma_{ij}(s)=\sum_{n\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\left(s^{2}\sqrt{\frac{b_{in}}{b_{jn}}}-s^{-2}\!\sqrt{\frac{b_{jn}}{b_{in}}}\right)^{2},\quad s\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\},

(4.79)

\displaystyle\Sigma_{ij}(C_{i},C_{j})=\sum_{n\in{\mathbb{Z}}}(C^{2}_{i}b_{in}+C^{2}_{j}b_{jn})(C_{i}a_{in}+C_{j}a_{jn})^{2}.

(4.80)

In this case there are four possibilities for the pair
(\Sigma^{12},\Sigma^{13})
:

(2.1)
(\Sigma^{12},\Sigma^{13})=(0,0)
, i.e.,
\Sigma^{12}<\infty
and
\Sigma^{13}<\infty
,

(2.2)
(\Sigma^{12},\Sigma^{13})=(0,1)
, i.e.,
\Sigma^{12}<\infty
, but
\Sigma^{13}=\infty
,

(2.3)
(\Sigma^{12},\Sigma^{13})=(1,0)
, i.e.,
\Sigma^{12}=\infty
, but
\Sigma^{13}<\infty
,

(2.4)
(\Sigma^{12},\Sigma^{13})=(1,1)
, i.e.,
\Sigma^{12}=\infty
and
\Sigma^{13}=\infty
.

Lemma 4.41
.

In the case
(2.1)
, i.e., when
(\Sigma^{12},\Sigma^{13})=(0,0)
, we can approximate
D_{rn}
for
1\leq r\leq 3
, hence the representation is irreducible.

Proof 20
.

Let
\Sigma^{12}<\infty
and
\Sigma^{13}<\infty
we have by (
4.47
)

\displaystyle\nu(C_{1},C_{2},C_{3})\sim\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}b_{1k}(C_{1}a_{1k}+C_{2}a_{2k}+C_{3}a_{3k})^{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(2.1)}}{{\sim}}\!\sum_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\left[\frac{t^{2}}{4}\frac{b_{1k}}{b_{2k}}\!+\!\frac{s^{2}}{4}\frac{b_{1k}}{b_{3k}}\!+\!\frac{b_{1k}}{2}(-2a_{1k}\!+\!ta_{2k}\!+\!sa_{3k})^{2}\right]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{S^{L}_{1,23}}}}{{=}}S^{L}_{1,23}(\mu,t,s)\!=\!\infty.

Hence,
D_{1n},\,D_{2n},\,D_{3n}\,\,\eta\,\,{\mathfrak{A}}^{3}
and the proof is finished.
∎

Remark 4.8
.

The cases (2.2) and (2.3) do not occur.

Indeed, by Lemma
4.36
the three series
\Sigma_{23}(s)

(defined by (
4.79
)),
\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}c_{n}^{2}
and

\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e_{n}^{2}

are equivalent where
\frac{s^{4}b_{2n}}{b_{3n}}=(1+c_{n})
, see Lemma
4.37
. In the case (c) we have
\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}c_{n}^{2}<\infty
, therefore,
\lim_{n}c_{n}=0
and hence,
\lim_{n}d_{n}^{-1}=\lim_{n}\frac{b_{2n}}{b_{3n}}=s^{-4}>0
. Recall that

d_{n}=\frac{d_{2n}}{d_{3n}}=\frac{b_{2n}}{b_{3n}}
.
But this contradicts
(\Sigma^{12},\Sigma^{13})=(0,1)
, or
(\Sigma^{12},\Sigma^{13})=(1,0)
, since the two series

\Sigma^{12}=\sum_{n}d_{2n}^{-1}\quad\text{and}\quad\Sigma^{13}=\sum_{n}d_{3n}^{-1}

should be equivalent by

\lim_{n}\frac{d_{2n}}{d_{3n}}=s^{-4}>0
.

In the
case (2.4)
we have

\Sigma_{23}(s)\!<\!\infty,\quad\Sigma_{23}(C_{2},C_{3})=\infty,\quad\Sigma^{12}=\Sigma^{13}=\infty.

(4.81)

To approximate
D_{rn}
we need to estimate
\nu(C_{1},C_{2},C_{3})
defined by (
4.45
).
By (
4.47
) we have

\nu(C_{1},C_{2},C_{3})\sim\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}b_{1n}\Big(\sum_{r=1}^{3}C_{r}a_{rn}\Big)^{2}.

Since
\|Y_{1}\|^{2}=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}b_{1n}a^{2}_{1n}\sim S^{L}_{11}(\mu)=\infty
, in the case (2.4) we have four possibilities
for
y_{23}:=(y_{2},y_{3})\in\{0,1\}^{2}
:

\begin{array}[]{ccccc}&(2.4.1)&(2.4.2)&(2.4.3)&(2.4.4)\\
y_{1}&1&1&1&1\\
y_{2}&0&1&0&1\\
y_{3}&0&0&1&1\\
\end{array}

Remark 4.9
.

The cases (2.4.1)–(2.4.3) are not compatible with the condition

\Sigma_{23}(C_{2},C_{3})=\infty
for all
(C_{2},C_{3})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\setminus\{0\}
.

So it suffices to consider only the
case (2.4.4)
when
y_{123}=(1,1,1)
.

The case (2.4.4) splits into two subcases:

(2.4.4.1) when
\Sigma_{12}(s_{12})\!<\!\infty
(resp.
\Sigma_{13}(s_{13})\!<\!\infty
) for some
s_{12},\,s_{13}>0
,

(2.4.4.2) when both
\Sigma_{12}(s_{12})\!=\!\Sigma_{13}(s_{13})=\infty
for all
s_{12},\,s_{13}>0
.

The case (2.4.4.1) does not occur.
Indeed, we have in this case
\Sigma_{13}(s_{12}s_{23})\!<\!\infty
(resp.
\Sigma_{12}(s_{13}s_{23}^{-1})\!<\!\infty
) since

\Sigma_{12}(s_{12})<\infty\Leftrightarrow\mu_{(s_{12}^{4}b_{1},0)}\sim\mu_{(b_{2},0)},\quad\Sigma_{23}(s_{23})<\infty\Leftrightarrow\mu_{(s_{23}^{4}b_{2},0)}\sim\mu_{(b_{3},0)}

where
\mu_{(b_{r},0)}\!=\!\otimes_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\mu_{(b_{rk},0)}

for
1\!\leq\!r\leq 3
. Therefore,

\mu_{((s_{12}s_{23})^{4}b_{1},0)}\sim\mu_{(b_{3},0)}\quad\Leftrightarrow\quad\Sigma_{13}(s_{12}s_{23})\!<\!\infty.

Similarly, if
\Sigma_{13}(s_{13})\!<\!\infty
and
\Sigma_{23}(s_{23})\!<\!\infty
we have

\mu_{(s_{13}^{4}b_{1},0)}\sim\mu_{(b_{3},0)},\quad\mu_{(s_{23}^{4}b_{2},0)}\sim\mu_{(b_{3},0)}\Rightarrow\mu_{\big((s_{13}s_{23}^{-1})^{4}b_{1},0\big)}\sim\mu_{(b_{2},0)}

hence,
\Sigma_{12}(s_{13}s_{23}^{-1})<\infty
.
But condition
\Sigma_{13}(s_{12}s_{23})+\Sigma_{12}(s_{13}s_{23}^{-1})<\infty
contradicts the first condition of (
4.39
).
Indeed, we have by Lemma
4.37

\displaystyle\Sigma_{12}(s)=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\left(s^{2}\sqrt{\frac{b_{1n}}{b_{2n}}}-s^{-2}\sqrt{\frac{b_{2n}}{b_{1n}}}\right)^{2}\sim\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}c_{n}^{2}<\infty,\quad s^{2}\sqrt{\frac{b_{1n}}{b_{2n}}}=1+c_{n},

\displaystyle\Sigma_{13}(s)=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\left(s^{2}\sqrt{\frac{b_{1n}}{b_{3n}}}-s^{-2}\sqrt{\frac{b_{3n}}{b_{1n}}}\right)^{2}\sim\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}f_{n}^{2}<\infty,\quad s^{2}\sqrt{\frac{b_{1n}}{b_{3n}}}=1+f_{n},

and
\lim_{n}c_{n}=\lim_{n}f_{n}=0
.
This contradicts

S_{1}(3)\sim\sum_{n}\frac{b_{1n}^{2}}{b_{2n}b_{3n}}<\infty
. Indeed,

\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{b_{1n}^{2}}{b_{2n}b_{3n}}=s^{-4}\lim_{n\to\infty}(1+c_{n})^{2}(1+f_{n})^{2}=s^{-4}>0.

Finally, to finish the case
S=(0,1,1)
, we need to consider only the case (2.4.4.2)
when
\Sigma_{12}(s_{12})\!=\!\Sigma_{13}(s_{13})=\infty
for all
s_{12},\,s_{13}>0
.

By (
4.39
) and all the previous considerations we have the conditions:

\displaystyle S_{1}(3)\!\sim\!\sum_{n}\frac{b_{1n}^{2}}{b_{2n}b_{3n}}\!<\!\infty,\quad S_{2}(3)\!\sim\!\sum_{n}\frac{b_{2n}}{b_{3n}}\!=\!\infty,\quad S_{3}(3)\!\sim\!\sum_{n}\frac{b_{3n}}{b_{2n}}\!=\!\infty,

\displaystyle\Sigma_{23}(C_{2},C_{3})=\infty,\quad\Sigma^{12}=\sum_{n}\frac{b_{1n}}{b_{2n}}=\infty,\quad\Sigma^{13}=\sum_{n}\frac{b_{1n}}{b_{3n}}=\infty,

(4.82)

\displaystyle\Sigma_{12}(s_{12})\!=\!\Sigma_{13}(s_{13})\!=\!\infty\,\,\text{for all}\,\,s_{12},\,s_{13}>0,\,\,\Sigma_{23}(s_{23})\!<\!\infty\,\,\text{for some}\,\,s_{23}>0.

Remark 4.10
.

By (
4.26
) without loss of generality we can suppose that
(b_{1n},b_{2n},b_{3n})
is replaced with
(1,d_{2n},d_{3n})
. Since
\Sigma_{23}(s)<\infty
, using notations
(
4.53
) and (
4.54
) of Lemma
4.36

\Sigma_{23}(s)\!=\!\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big(\frac{s^{2}}{\sqrt{d_{n}}}\!-\!\frac{\sqrt{d_{n}}}{s^{2}}\Big)^{2}\!=\!\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big(s^{2}\sqrt{\frac{d_{2n}}{d_{3n}}}\!-\!s^{-2}\sqrt{\frac{d_{3n}}{d_{2n}}}\Big)^{2},

and taking into consideration (
4.82
), we can chose
d_{2n}
and
d_{3n}
as follows:

d_{n}\!=\!\frac{d_{3n}}{d_{2n}}\!=\!s^{4}(1+c_{n}),\,\,\,\sum_{n}c^{2}_{n}<\infty,\,\,\,\sum_{n}\frac{1}{d^{2}_{2n}}<\infty,\,\,\,\,\,\sum_{n}\frac{1}{d_{n}}=\sum_{n}d_{n}\!=\!\infty.

(4.83)

Since
\sum_{n}c^{2}_{n}<\infty

we have

\sum_{n}\frac{b_{1n}^{2}}{b_{2n}b_{3n}}\sim\sum_{n}\frac{1}{d^{2}_{2n}}
and
the measures

\mu_{(d^{c,s}_{3},0)}
and
\mu_{(d^{s}_{3},0)}
are equivalent, where

\mu_{(d^{c,s}_{3},0)}=\otimes_{n}\mu_{(s^{4}d_{2n}(1+c_{n}),0)},\quad\mu_{(d^{s}_{3},0)}=\otimes_{n}\mu_{(s^{4}d_{2n},0)},

hence, we can choose
c_{n}\equiv 0
and
s=1
.
So, to finish the case
S=(0,1,1)
we should prove the irreducibility for

b=(1,d_{2n},d_{2n})_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}

with the only condition:

\sum_{n}d^{-2}_{2n}<\infty.\quad\text{Since}\quad d_{n}\equiv 1,\quad\text{we have}\quad\sum_{n}d^{-1}_{n}=\sum_{n}d_{n}\!=\!\infty.

(4.84)

As usual,
a=(a_{1n},a_{2n},a_{3n})_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}
should satisfy the orthogonality condition:

(\mu_{(b,a)}^{3})^{L_{t}}\perp\mu_{(b,a)}^{3}\quad\text{for all}\quad t\in{\rm GL}(3,{\mathbb{R}})\setminus\{e\}.

Example 4.2
.

The pairwise conditions

\|C_{r}Y_{r}+C_{s}Y_{s}\|^{2}=\infty\,\,\text{ for}\,\,1\leq r<s\leq 3\quad\text{do not imply}\quad\|\sum_{r=1}^{3}C_{r}Y_{r}\|^{2}=\infty.

Let
a_{r,n}=a_{r,-n}
for
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
a_{1,0}=1,\,\,a_{2,0}=2,\,\,a_{3,0}=3
.
We define
a_{r,n}
for
n\in\mathbb{N}
as follows

a_{1n}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}2&n=2k+1\\
1&n=2k\end{array}\right.,\quad a_{2n}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}1&n=2k+1\\
2&n=2k\end{array}\right.,\quad a_{3n}\equiv 3.

(4.85)

Then we have clearly for arbitrary
(C_{1},C_{2},C_{3})\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\setminus\{0\}

\displaystyle\|C_{1}a_{1}\!+\!C_{2}a_{2}\|^{2}\!=\!\infty,\,\,\,\,\|C_{1}a_{1}\!+\!C_{3}a_{3}\|^{2}\!=\!\infty,\,\,\|C_{2}a_{2}\!+\!C_{3}a_{3}\|^{2}\!=\!\infty,\qquad

(4.86)

\displaystyle\text{but}\quad a_{1}+a_{2}-a_{3}=0\quad\text{hence,}\quad\|a_{1}+a_{2}-a_{3}\|^{2}=0.

(4.87)

Example 4.3
.

(4.88)

Lemma 4.42
.

In Example
4.2
we have
(
we consider only
n\in\mathbb{N}
)

\Delta(a_{1},a_{2},a_{3})=2,\quad\Delta(a_{2},a_{3},a_{1})=2,\quad\Delta(a_{3},a_{1},a_{2})=2,

(4.89)

where
a_{r}=(a_{rn})_{n\in\mathbb{N}},\,\,1\leq r\leq 3
.

Proof 21
.

Set
a_{r}(n)=(a_{rl})_{l=1}^{n}
for
1\leq r\leq 3
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
, then for
1\leq k<r\leq 3

\Gamma(a_{k}(n))\!\sim\!\Gamma(a_{1}(n)+a_{2}(n))\!\sim\!n,\,\,\,\Gamma(a_{k}(n),a_{r}(n))\!\sim\!\frac{n(n-1)}{2},\,\,\,\Gamma(a_{1},a_{2},a_{3})\!=\!0.

We observe that
\Gamma(a_{k},a_{k}+a_{r})=\Gamma(a_{k},a_{r})
for
k\not=r
. Since
a_{3}=a_{1}+a_{2}
we get

\displaystyle\Delta(a_{1},a_{2},a_{3})=\frac{\Gamma(a_{1})+\Gamma(a_{1},a_{2})+\Gamma(a_{1},a_{3})+\Gamma(a_{1},a_{2},a_{3})}{1+\Gamma(a_{2})+\Gamma(a_{3})+\Gamma(a_{2},a_{3})}=

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma(a_{1})+\Gamma(a_{1},a_{2})+\Gamma(a_{1},a_{1}+a_{2})+\Gamma(a_{1},a_{2},a_{1}+a_{2})}{1+\Gamma(a_{2})+\Gamma(a_{1}+a_{2})+\Gamma(a_{2},a_{1}+a_{2})}=

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma(a_{1})+2\Gamma(a_{1},a_{2})}{1+\Gamma(a_{2})+\Gamma(a_{1}+a_{2})+\Gamma(a_{1},a_{2})}=2,

\displaystyle\Delta(a_{2},a_{3},a_{1})=\frac{\Gamma(a_{2})+\Gamma(a_{2},a_{3})+\Gamma(a_{2},a_{1})+\Gamma(a_{2},a_{3},a_{1})}{1+\Gamma(a_{3})+\Gamma(a_{1})+\Gamma(a_{3},a_{1})}=

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma(a_{2})+\Gamma(a_{2},a_{1}+a_{2})+\Gamma(a_{2},a_{1})+\Gamma(a_{2},a_{1}+a_{2},a_{1})}{1+\Gamma(a_{1}+a_{2})+\Gamma(a_{1})+\Gamma(a_{1}+a_{2},a_{1})}=

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma(a_{2})+2\Gamma(a_{2},a_{1})}{1+\Gamma(a_{1}+a_{2})+\Gamma(a_{1})+\Gamma(a_{2},a_{1})}=2,

\displaystyle\Delta(a_{3},a_{1},a_{2})=\frac{\Gamma(a_{3})+\Gamma(a_{3},a_{1})+\Gamma(a_{3},a_{2})+\Gamma(a_{3},a_{1},a_{2})}{1+\Gamma(a_{1})+\Gamma(a_{2})+\Gamma(a_{1},a_{2})}=

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma(a_{1}+a_{2})+\Gamma(a_{1}+a_{2},a_{1})+\Gamma(a_{1}+a_{2},a_{2})+\Gamma(a_{1}+a_{2},a_{1},a_{2})}{1+\Gamma(a_{1})+\Gamma(a_{2})+\Gamma(a_{1},a_{2})}=

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma(a_{1}+a_{2})+2\Gamma(a_{1},a_{2})}{1+\Gamma(a_{1})+\Gamma(a_{2})+\Gamma(a_{1},a_{2})}=2.

We use two facts for
1\leq r\leq 2
:

\frac{\Gamma(a_{1},a_{2})}{\Gamma(a_{r})}=\infty\quad\text{and}\quad\Gamma(a_{1}+a_{2})\leq\Gamma(a_{1})+\Gamma(a_{2})+2\sqrt{\Gamma(a_{1})\Gamma(a_{2})}.

The first relation follows from Lemma
8.83
since

\|C_{1}a_{1}+C_{2}a_{2}\|^{2}=\infty
. We get

\frac{\Gamma(a_{1},a_{2})}{\Gamma(a_{r})}=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{\Gamma(a_{1}(n),a_{2}(n))}{\Gamma(a_{r}(n))}=\infty.

Recall that
\Gamma(a)=\|a\|^{2}
.
The inequality follows from
\|a_{1}+a_{2}\|\leq\|a_{1}\|+\|a_{2}\|
, i.e.,

\sqrt{\Gamma(a_{1}+a_{2})}\leq\sqrt{\Gamma(a_{1})}+\sqrt{\Gamma(a_{2})}
.
∎

By Lemma‘
2.10
we have

(\mu_{(b,a)}^{3})^{L_{t}}\perp\mu_{(b,a)}^{3}\quad\Leftrightarrow\quad\Sigma^{\pm}(t):=\Sigma_{1}^{\pm}(t)+\Sigma_{2}(t)=\infty,

where

\Sigma_{1}^{+}(t)\!=\!\sum_{n\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq 3}\Big(t^{i}_{j}\sqrt{\frac{b_{in}}{b_{jn}}}\!-\!A^{i}_{j}(t)\sqrt{\frac{b_{jn}}{b_{in}}}\Big)^{2},\quad

\Sigma_{1}^{-}(t)\!=\!\sum_{n\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq 3}\Big(t^{i}_{j}\sqrt{\frac{b_{in}}{b_{jn}}}\!+\!A^{i}_{j}(t)\sqrt{\frac{b_{jn}}{b_{in}}}\Big)^{2},

\displaystyle\Sigma_{2}(t^{-1})=\sum_{n\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\Big[b_{1n}\big((t_{11}-1)a_{1n}+t_{12}a_{2n}+t_{13}a_{3n}\big)^{2}+

\displaystyle b_{2n}\big(t_{21}a_{1n}+(t_{22}\!-\!1)a_{2n}+t_{23}a_{3n}\big)^{2}+b_{3n}\big(t_{31}a_{1n}+t_{32}a_{2n}+(t_{33}\!-\!1)a_{3n}\big)^{2}\Big].

In Example
4.3
we can not approximate

x_{2n},\,x_{3n}
since in this case we have

\Delta(Y_{2}^{(2)},Y_{3}^{(2)})=1,\quad\Delta(Y_{3}^{(3)},Y_{2}^{(3)})\!=1.

(4.90)

Indeed, by (
4.43
) we have

\Delta(Y_{2}^{(2)},Y_{3}^{(2)})=\!\frac{\Gamma(Y_{2}^{(2)})\!+\!\Gamma(Y_{2}^{(2)},Y_{3}^{(2)})}{1+\Gamma(Y_{3}^{(2)})},\,\,\Delta(Y_{3}^{(3)},Y_{2}^{(3)})=\frac{\Gamma(Y_{3}^{(3)})\!+\!\Gamma(Y_{3}^{(3)},Y_{2}^{(3)})}{1+\Gamma(Y_{2}^{(3)})}.

(4.91)

In Example
4.3
we have

d_{n}=\frac{d_{3n}}{d_{2n}}\equiv 1

and hence, by (
4.49
)
we have

\displaystyle\|Y_{2}^{(2)}\|^{2}\sim\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{1}{1+2d_{n}}=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{1}{3},\quad\|Y_{3}^{(2)}\|^{2}\sim\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{d_{n}^{2}}{1+2d_{n}}=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{1}{3},

\displaystyle\|Y_{2}^{(3)}\|^{2}\sim\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{1}{d_{n}^{2}+2d_{n}}=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{1}{3},\quad\|Y_{3}^{(3)}\|^{2}\sim\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{d_{n}^{2}}{d_{n}^{2}+2d_{n}}=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{1}{3}.

Therefore,

\Gamma(Y_{2}^{(2)},Y_{3}^{(2)})=\Gamma(Y_{3}^{(3)},Y_{2}^{(3)})=0,

and

\Delta(Y_{2}^{(2)},Y_{3}^{(2)})=\!\frac{\Gamma(Y_{2}^{(2)})}{1+\Gamma(Y_{3}^{(2)})}=1,\quad\Delta(Y_{3}^{(3)},Y_{2}^{(3)})=\frac{\Gamma(Y_{3}^{(3)})}{1+\Gamma(Y_{2}^{(3)})}=1.

Since

, by (

) we get

so by (

) we have

\nu(C_{1},C_{2},C_{3})\sim\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}b_{1n}\Big(\sum_{r=1}^{3}C_{r}a_{rn}\Big)^{2}=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big(\sum_{r=1}^{3}C_{r}a_{rn}\Big)^{2}.

But in Example

.

4.4.9
Approximations of
x_{2k}x_{2r}+x_{3k}x_{3r}
in the case (c)

Since we can not approximate
x_{2n}x_{2t},\,x_{3n}x_{3t}
using Lemmas
5.53
–
5.54
in the case (c), we shall try to approximate

x_{2k}x_{2r}+s^{4}x_{3k}x_{3r}

by an appropriate combinations of
A_{kn}A_{rn}
for
n\in\mathbb{Z}
. Let
s=1
, the general case is similar.

Lemma 4.43
.

For any
k,r\in{\mathbb{Z}}
one has

\displaystyle(x_{2k}x_{2r}\!+\!x_{3k}x_{3r}){\bf 1}\in\langle A_{kn}A_{rn}{\bf 1}\mid n\in{\mathbb{Z}}\rangle\,\,\Leftrightarrow\,\,\Delta(Y^{(2)},Y^{(1)})\!=\!\infty,

(4.92)

\displaystyle\text{where}\quad Y^{(r)}\!=\!\Big(\frac{b_{rn}}{\sqrt{\lambda_{n}}}\Big)_{n\in\mathbb{Z}},\,\,\,1\leq r\leq 2,\,\,\,\lambda_{n}\!=\!(b_{1n}+b_{2n}+b_{3n})^{2}-b^{2}_{1n}.

Proof 22
.

The proof of Lemma
4.43
is based on Lemma
7.71
.
We study when

(x_{2k}x_{2r}+x_{3k}x_{3r}){\bf 1}\in\langle A_{kn}A_{rn}{\bf 1}\mid n\in{\mathbb{Z}}\rangle.

Since

\displaystyle A_{kn}A_{rn}

\displaystyle=(x_{1k}D_{1n}+x_{2k}D_{2n}+x_{3k}D_{3n})(x_{1r}D_{1n}+x_{2r}D_{2n}+x_{3r}D_{3n})

\displaystyle=x_{1k}x_{1r}D_{1n}^{2}+x_{2k}x_{2r}D_{2n}^{2}+x_{3k}x_{3r}D_{3n}^{2}+(x_{1k}x_{2r}+x_{2k}x_{1r})D_{1n}D_{2n}

\displaystyle+(x_{1k}x_{3r}+x_{3k}x_{1r})D_{1n}D_{3n}+(x_{2k}x_{3r}+x_{3k}x_{2r})D_{2n}D_{3n},

and
MD_{rn}^{2}{\bf 1}=-\frac{b_{rn}}{2}
, for
2\leq r\leq 3
we take
t=(t_{n})_{n=-m}^{m}
as follows:

(t,b_{2})\!=\!(t,b_{3})\!=\!1,\,\,\text{where}\,\,t\!=\!(t_{n})_{k=-m}^{m},\,\,b_{2}\!=\!-(b_{2n}/2)_{n=-m}^{m},\,\,b_{3}\!=\!-(b_{3n}/2)_{n=-m}^{m}.

We have

\displaystyle\|\big[\sum_{n=-m}^{m}t_{n}A_{kn}A_{rn}-\big(x_{2k}x_{2r}+x_{3k}x_{3r}\big)\big]{\bf 1}\|^{2}=

\displaystyle\|\!\!\sum_{n=-m}^{m}t_{n}\big[x_{1k}x_{1r}D_{1n}^{2}\!+\!x_{2k}x_{2r}\big(D_{2n}^{2}\!+\!\frac{b_{2n}}{2}\big)\!+\!x_{3k}x_{3r}\big(D_{3n}^{2}\!+\!\frac{b_{3n}}{2}\big)+(x_{1k}x_{2r}+

\displaystyle x_{2k}x_{1r})D_{1n}D_{2n}\!+\!(x_{1k}x_{3r}+x_{3k}x_{1r})D_{1n}D_{3n}\!+\!(x_{2k}x_{3r}+x_{3k}x_{2r})D_{2n}D_{3n}\Big]{\bf 1}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{-m\leq n,l\leq m}(f_{n},f_{l})t_{n}t_{l}=:(A_{2m+1}t,t),

where
A_{2m+1}=(f_{n},f_{l})_{n,l=-m}^{m}
and

\displaystyle f_{n}=\sum_{i=1}^{3}f_{n}^{i}+\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq 3}f_{n}^{ij},\quad\text{with}

(4.93)

\displaystyle f_{n}^{i}=x_{ik}x_{ir}\Big(D_{in}^{2}+\frac{b_{in}}{2}(1-\delta_{i1})\Big){\bf 1},\quad f_{n}^{ij}=(x_{ik}x_{jr}+x_{jk}x_{ir})D_{in}D_{jn}{\bf 1}

for
1\leq i\leq 3,\,\,1\leq i<j\leq 3
.
Since

f_{n}^{i^{\prime}}\perp f_{n}^{ij},\quad f_{n}^{ij}\perp f_{n}^{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}}

for different
(ij),\,\,(i^{\prime}j^{\prime})
,
writing
c_{kn}=\|x_{kn}\|^{2}=\frac{1}{2b_{kn}}+a_{kn}^{2}
, we get

\displaystyle(f_{n},f_{n})=\sum_{i=1}^{3}\|f_{n}^{i}\|^{2}+\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq 3}\|f_{n}^{ij}\|^{2}=

\displaystyle c_{1k}c_{1r}3\Big(\frac{b_{1n}}{2}\Big)^{2}+c_{2k}c_{2r}2\Big(\frac{b_{2n}}{2}\Big)^{2}+c_{3k}c_{3r}2\Big(\frac{b_{3n}}{2}\Big)^{2}+

\displaystyle\big(c_{1k}c_{2r}+c_{2k}c_{1r}+2a_{1k}a_{2r}a_{2k}a_{1r}\big)\frac{b_{1n}}{2}\frac{b_{2n}}{2}+\big(c_{1k}c_{3r}+c_{3k}c_{1r}+2a_{1k}a_{3r}a_{3k}a_{1r}\big)

\displaystyle\times\frac{b_{1n}}{2}\frac{b_{3n}}{2}+\big(c_{2k}c_{3r}+c_{3k}c_{2r}+2a_{2k}a_{3r}a_{3k}a_{2r}\big)\frac{b_{2n}}{2}\frac{b_{3n}}{2}\sim(b_{1n}+b_{2n}+b_{3n})^{2},

\displaystyle(f_{n},f_{l})=(f_{n}^{1},f_{l}^{1})=c_{1k}c_{1r}\frac{b_{1n}}{2}\frac{b_{1l}}{2}\sim b_{1n}b_{1l}.

Finally, we get

(f_{n},f_{n})\sim(b_{1n}+b_{2n}+b_{3n})^{2},\quad(f_{n},f_{l})\sim b_{1n}b_{1l},\quad n\not=l.

(4.94)

Set

\lambda_{n}=(b_{1n}+b_{2n}+b_{3n})^{2}-b_{1n}^{2},\quad g_{n}=(b_{1n}),

(4.95)

then

(f_{n},f_{n})\sim\lambda_{n}+(g_{n},g_{n}),\quad(f_{n},f_{l})\sim(g_{n},g_{l}).

(4.96)

For
A_{2m+1}=\big((f_{n},f_{l})\big)_{n,l=-m}^{m}
and

b_{2}=b_{3}=-(b_{2n}/2)_{n=-m}^{m}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2m+1}
we have

A_{2m+1}=\sum_{n=-m}^{m}\lambda_{n}E_{nn}+\gamma(g_{-m},\dots,g_{0},\dots,g_{m}).

To finish the proof, it suffices to use Lemma
7.71
.
∎

Remark 4.11
.

In the case (c) we can approximate
x_{2k}x_{2r}+x_{3k}x_{3r}
since
\Delta(Y^{(2)},Y^{(1)})\!=\!\infty
.

Indeed, by (
4.92
) we have

\displaystyle\Delta(Y^{(2)},Y^{(1)})=\frac{\Gamma(Y^{(2)})\!+\!\Gamma(Y^{(2)},Y^{(1)})}{1+\Gamma(Y^{(1)})}>\frac{\Gamma(Y^{(2)})}{1+\Gamma(Y^{(1)})}=

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{b^{2}_{2n}}{\lambda_{n}}}{1+\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{b^{2}_{1n}}{\lambda_{n}}}=\frac{\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{d^{2}_{2n}}{(1+2d_{2n})^{2}-1}}{1+\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{1}{(1+2d_{2n})^{2}-1}}\sim\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{d^{2}_{2n}}{d_{n}+d^{2}_{2n}}=\infty

since by (
4.83
) we have
\sum_{n}\frac{1}{d^{2}_{2n}}<\infty
. Therefore,

\Gamma(Y^{(1)})=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{1}{(1+2d_{2n})^{2}-1}\sim\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{1}{d_{n}+d^{2}_{2n}}<\infty.

Lemma 4.44
.

We have for all
k\in\mathbb{Z}

x_{2k}{\bf 1}\in\langle(x_{2k}x_{2n}+x_{3k}x_{3n}){\bf 1}\mid n\in{\mathbb{Z}}\rangle\,\,\Leftrightarrow\,\,\sigma_{2}(\mu)=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{a_{2n}^{2}}{\frac{1}{2b_{2n}}+\frac{1}{2b_{3n}}+a_{3n}^{2}}=\infty.

(4.97)

Proof 23
.

Recall the notation
c_{rn}=\frac{1}{2b_{rn}}+a_{rn}^{2}
.
Since
Mx_{2n}{\bf 1}=a_{2n}
we take
t=(t_{n})_{n=-m}^{m}
as follows:
(t,a_{2})=1
, where
a_{2}=(a_{2n})_{n=-m}^{m}.

We have

\displaystyle\|\big[\sum_{n=-m}^{m}t_{n}\big(x_{2k}x_{2n}+x_{3k}x_{3n}\big)-x_{2k}x_{2n}\big]{\bf 1}\|^{2}=

\displaystyle\|\big[\sum_{n=-m}^{m}t_{n}\big(x_{2k}(x_{2n}-a_{2n})+x_{3k}x_{3n}\big)\big]{\bf 1}\|^{2}=\|x_{2k}{\bf 1}\|^{2}\|\sum_{n=-m}^{m}t_{n}(x_{2n}-a_{2n}){\bf 1}\|^{2}

\displaystyle+\|x_{3k}{\bf 1}\|^{2}\|\sum_{n=-m}^{m}t_{n}x_{3n}{\bf 1}\|^{2}=c_{2k}\sum_{n=-m}^{m}t^{2}_{n}\frac{1}{2b_{2n}}+c_{3k}\sum_{n=-m}^{m}t^{2}_{n}\Big(\frac{1}{2b_{3n}}+a^{2}_{3n}\Big)

\displaystyle\sim\sum_{n=-m}^{m}t^{2}_{n}\Big(\frac{1}{2b_{2n}}+\frac{1}{2b_{3n}}+a^{2}_{3n}\Big).

By (
6.3
) we get (
4.97
).
∎

4.108
) we get

\lambda_{k}=-\frac{2s^{2}_{k}C_{k}}{s^{2}_{1}C^{2}_{1}+s^{2}_{2}C^{2}_{2}+s^{2}_{3}C^{2}_{3}},\quad 1\leq k\leq 3.

(4.109)

Then the matrix elements
t=(t_{kr})_{k,r=1}^{3}
are defined by (
4.107
). To verify
e_{k}\perp e_{r}
we need to show that

\displaystyle(e_{1},e_{2})=\frac{(s^{2}_{1}C^{2}_{1}+s^{2}_{2}C^{2}_{2}+s^{2}_{3}C^{2}_{3})\lambda_{1}\lambda_{2}}{s_{1}s_{2}}+\frac{s^{2}_{1}C_{1}\lambda_{2}+s^{2}_{2}C_{2}\lambda_{1}}{s_{1}s_{2}}=0,

\displaystyle(e_{1},e_{3})=\frac{(s^{2}_{1}C^{2}_{1}+s^{2}_{2}C^{2}_{2}+s^{2}_{3}C^{2}_{3})\lambda_{1}\lambda_{3}}{s_{1}s_{3}}+\frac{s^{2}_{1}C_{1}\lambda_{3}+s^{2}_{3}C_{3}\lambda_{1}}{s_{1}s_{3}}=0,

\displaystyle(e_{2},e_{3})=\frac{(s^{2}_{1}C^{2}_{1}+s^{2}_{2}C^{2}_{2}+s^{2}_{3}C^{2}_{3})\lambda_{2}\lambda_{3}}{s_{2}s_{3}}+\frac{s^{2}_{2}C_{2}\lambda_{3}+s^{2}_{3}C_{2}\lambda_{3}}{s_{2}s_{3}}=0.

Indeed, for example, for
(e_{1},e_{2})
we have

\displaystyle(e_{1},e_{2})=\frac{(s^{2}_{1}C^{2}_{1}+s^{2}_{2}C^{2}_{2}+s^{2}_{3}C^{2}_{3})\lambda_{1}\lambda_{2}}{s_{1}s_{2}}+\frac{s^{2}_{1}C_{1}\lambda_{2}+s^{2}_{2}C_{2}\lambda_{1}}{s_{1}s_{2}}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{s_{1}s_{2}(s^{2}_{1}C_{1}^{2}+s^{2}_{2}C_{2}^{2}+s^{2}_{3}C_{3}^{2})}\Big(4s^{2}_{1}s^{2}_{2}-(2s^{2}_{1}s^{2}_{2}+2s^{2}_{1}s^{2}_{2})\Big)C_{1}C_{2}=0.

The proofs of
e_{1}\perp e_{3}
and
e_{2}\perp e_{3}
are similar.
∎

Similarly, for any
m\geq 2
we can prove the following lemma:

Lemma 4.48
.

For an arbitrary
(C_{k})_{k=1}^{m}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{m}\setminus\{0\}
, and
D_{m}(s)={\rm diag}(s_{k})_{k=1}^{m}
with

s_{k}\in\mathbb{R}_{+},\,\,1\leq k\leq m

there exists a unique element
t\!\in\!\pm{\rm O}(m)\setminus\{e\}
and
(\lambda_{k})_{k=1}^{m}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{m}\setminus\{0\}

such that

D_{m}(s)tD^{-1}_{m}(s)-{\rm I}=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\lambda_{1}C_{1}&\lambda_{1}C_{2}&\dots&\lambda_{1}C_{m}\\
\lambda_{2}C_{1}&\lambda_{2}C_{2}&\dots&\lambda_{2}C_{m}\\
&&\dots&&\\
\lambda_{m}C_{1}&\lambda_{m}C_{2}&\dots&\lambda_{m}C_{m}\end{smallmatrix}\right).

(4.110)

The formulas for the corresponding
\lambda_{k}
are as follows:

\lambda_{k}=-\frac{2s^{2}_{k}C_{k}}{\sum_{r=1}^{m}s^{2}_{r}C^{2}_{r}},\quad 1\leq k\leq m.

(4.111)

Lemma 4.49
.

For an arbitrary
(C_{1},C_{2})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\setminus\{0\}
and arbitrary
D_{2}(s)={\rm diag}(s_{1},s_{2})
with
s_{1},s_{2}\not=0

there exists a unique element
t\!\in\!\pm{\rm O}(2)\setminus\{e\}
and
(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\setminus\{0\}

such that

D_{2}(s)tD^{-1}_{2}(s)-{\rm I}=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\lambda_{1}C_{1}&\lambda_{1}C_{2}\\
\lambda_{2}C_{1}&\lambda_{2}C_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right).

(4.112)

The formulas for the corresponding
\lambda_{k}
are as follows:

\lambda_{1}=-\frac{2s_{1}^{2}C_{1}}{s_{1}^{2}C^{2}_{1}+s_{2}^{2}C^{2}_{2}},\quad\lambda_{2}=-\frac{2s_{2}C_{2}}{s_{1}^{2}C^{2}_{1}+s_{2}^{2}C^{2}_{2}}.

(4.113)

In particular, we have

\left(\begin{smallmatrix}t_{11}&\frac{s_{1}}{s_{2}}t_{12}\\
\frac{s_{2}}{s_{1}}t_{21}&t_{22}\end{smallmatrix}\right)=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}C_{1}\lambda_{1}+1&C_{2}\lambda_{1}\\
C_{1}\lambda_{2}&C_{2}\lambda_{2}+1\end{smallmatrix}\right)=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\frac{s_{2}^{2}C^{2}_{2}-s^{2}C^{2}_{1}}{s_{1}^{2}C^{2}_{1}+s_{2}^{2}C^{2}_{2}}&-\frac{2s_{1}^{2}C_{1}C_{2}}{s_{1}^{2}C^{2}_{1}+s_{2}^{2}C^{2}_{2}}\\
-\frac{2s_{2}^{2}C_{1}C_{2}}{s_{1}^{2}C^{2}_{1}+s_{2}^{2}C^{2}_{2}}&\frac{s_{1}^{2}C^{2}_{1}-s_{2}^{2}C^{2}_{2}}{s_{1}^{2}C^{2}_{1}+s_{2}^{2}C^{2}_{2}}\end{smallmatrix}\right)=\tau_{12}(\phi,s_{1},s_{2}).

We can verify that

\tau_{12}(\phi,s,s^{-1})=\tau_{-}(\phi,s)

where
\tau_{-}(\phi,s)
is defined by (
2.4
).
We just set

\cos\phi=\frac{s^{-2}C^{2}_{2}-s^{2}C^{2}_{1}}{s^{2}C^{2}_{1}+s^{-2}C^{2}_{2}}\quad\text{and}\quad\sin\phi=-\frac{2C_{1}C_{2}}{s^{2}C^{2}_{1}+s^{-2}C^{2}_{2}}.

In addition
{\rm det}\,t=-1
.

\displaystyle\text{therefore, we can take}\quad b=(1,1,b_{3n})_{n\in\mathbb{Z}},\,\,s=1,\,\,c_{n}\equiv 0.

Since

(4.114)

where

\displaystyle S_{2}(3)\!=\!\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{1}{1\!+\!2b_{3n}}\!=\!\infty,\,\,\,\,\infty\!=\!S_{3}(3)\!=\!\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{b_{3n}^{2}}{1\!+\!2b_{3n}}\!\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{two-ser-alpha}}}{{\sim}}\!\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}b_{3n}^{2}.\qquad\,\,

(4.115)

\displaystyle\|Y_{r}^{(s)}\|^{2}=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{b_{rk}^{2}}{b_{sk}^{2}+2(b_{1n}b_{2n}+b_{1n}b_{3n}+b_{2n}b_{3n})},\,\,s\not=r.

(4.116)

We have

Lemma 4.50
.

In the cases (a), (b) and (c) given by (

(4.117)

Proof 27

\displaystyle=\frac{\Gamma(Y_{3}^{(3)})+2\Gamma(Y_{3}^{(3)},Y_{1}^{(3)})}{1+2\Gamma(Y_{1}^{(3)})}\sim\Delta(Y_{3}^{(3)},Y_{1}^{(3)}).

(4.118)

(4.119)

Then this will imply (
4.117

by Lemma
4.39
,
contradiction with (
4.122
) hence,
D_{3n}\,\eta\,{\mathfrak{A}}^{3}
.

4.5.3
Case
\Sigma_{123}(s)=(1,1,1)

We have for all
s=(s_{12},s_{23},s_{13})\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{3}\setminus\{0\}

\displaystyle\Sigma_{12}(s_{12})=\infty,\quad\Sigma_{23}(s_{23})=\infty,\quad\Sigma_{13}(s_{13})=\infty,

(4.125)

\displaystyle b\!=\!(b_{1n},b_{2n},b_{3n})_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{(b,a)-(b',a')}}}{{=}}\!(1,d_{2n},d_{3n})_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}.

Recall (
4.40
), that we denote
D_{n}:=d^{-1}_{2n}+d^{-1}_{3n}+1
and
d_{n}=\frac{d_{3n}}{d_{3n}}
. Set

\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}Y_{1n}^{(1)}&Y_{2n}^{(1)}&Y_{3n}^{(1)}\\
Y_{1n}^{(2)}&Y_{2n}^{(2)}&Y_{3n}^{(2)}\\
Y_{1n}^{(3)}&Y_{2n}^{(3)}&Y_{3n}^{(3)}\\
\end{array}\right)=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\frac{1}{\sqrt{1\!+\!2D_{n}d_{2n}d_{3n}}}&\frac{d_{2n}}{\sqrt{1\!+\!2D_{n}d_{2n}d_{3n}}}&\frac{d_{3n}}{\sqrt{1\!+\!2D_{n}d_{2n}d_{3n}}}\\
\frac{1}{\sqrt{d_{2n}^{2}\!+\!2D_{n}d_{2n}d_{3n}}}&\frac{d_{2n}}{\sqrt{d_{2n}^{2}\!+\!2D_{n}d_{2n}d_{3n}}}&\frac{d_{3n}}{\sqrt{d_{2n}^{2}\!+\!2D_{n}d_{2n}d_{3n}}}\\
\frac{1}{\sqrt{d_{3n}^{2}\!+\!2D_{n}d_{2n}d_{3n}}}&\frac{d_{2n}}{\sqrt{d_{3n}^{2}\!+\!2D_{n}d_{2n}d_{3n}}}&\frac{d_{3n}}{\sqrt{d_{3n}^{2}\!+\!2D_{n}d_{2n}d_{3n}}}\end{array}\right).

(4.126)

Remark 4.16
.

For
(r,s)
such that
1\leq r<s\leq 3
the following equivalence hold

\displaystyle\Sigma_{rs}(s_{rs})\!<\!\infty\,\,\Leftrightarrow\,\,\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}c^{2}_{rs,n}\!<\!\infty\,\,\Leftrightarrow\,\,\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}c^{2}_{sr,n}\!<\!\infty,\,\,\text{where}

(4.127)

\displaystyle\frac{b_{rn}}{b_{sn}}=:s^{-4}_{rs}(1+c_{rs,n}),\,\,\frac{b_{sn}}{b_{rn}}\!=\!s^{4}_{rs}(1\!+\!c_{sr,n}),\,\,\lim_{n}\frac{b_{rn}}{b_{sn}}\in(0,\infty).

(4.128)

Proof 28
.

By Lemma
8.74
we have

\displaystyle\Sigma_{rs}(s_{rs})=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big(s_{rs}^{2}\sqrt{\frac{b_{rn}}{b_{sn}}}\!-\!s_{rs}^{-2}\sqrt{\frac{b_{sn}}{b_{rn}}}\Big)^{2}=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{c^{2}_{rs,n}}{1+c_{rs,n}}\sim\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}c^{2}_{rs,n},

\displaystyle\Sigma_{sr}(s^{-1}_{rs})=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big(s_{rs}^{-2}\sqrt{\frac{b_{sn}}{b_{rn}}}\!-\!s_{rs}^{2}\sqrt{\frac{b_{rn}}{b_{sn}}}\Big)^{2}=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{c^{2}_{sr,n}}{1+c_{sr,n}}\sim\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}c^{2}_{sr,n},

\displaystyle\text{note also that}\quad 1=\frac{b_{rn}}{b_{sn}}\frac{b_{sn}}{b_{rn}}=(1\!+\!c_{rs,n})(1\!+\!c_{sr,n}).

(4.129)

∎

By Remark
4.16
, the condition
\Sigma_{rs}(s_{rs})=\infty
means the following:

l_{sr}:=\lim_{n}\frac{b_{sn}}{b_{rn}}=\!\left\{\begin{array}[]{lccc}(a)&\infty&&\\
(b)&s^{4}_{rs}>0&\text{with}&\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}c^{2}_{sr,n}=\infty.\\
(c)&0&\\
(d)&\lim&\,\,\text{does not exist}\end{array}\right.

(4.130)

Remark 4.17
.

In the case (d) we can use the fact that some subsequence of
\Big(\frac{b_{sn}}{b_{rn}}\Big)_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}
has property (a), (b) or (c).
We can avoid the case (c). Namely, if
l_{sr}=0
for some pair
(r,s)
with
1\leq r<s\leq 3
, we can exchange the two lines
(b_{sn},a_{sn})
and
(b_{rn},a_{rn})
to obtain
l_{sr}=\infty
.

Formally, we have
3^{3}=\#(A)^{\#(B)}
possibilities where
A=\{(21),(32),(31)\}
and
B=\{(a),(b),(d)\}
. Since
l_{32}l_{21}=l_{31}
we get only the following cases:

\begin{array}[]{ccccc}e\setminus(rs)&(21)&(32)&(31)\\
(1)&b&b&b\\
(2)&a&a&a\\
(3)&a&b&a\\
(4)&b&a&b\\
\end{array}.

To be able to approximate
x_{rn}
for
1\leq r\leq 3
we should study when the following expressions are infinite:

\rho_{r}(C_{1},C_{2},C_{3})=\|C_{1}Y_{1}^{(r)}+C_{2}Y_{2}^{(r)}+C_{2}Y_{3}^{(r)}\|^{2}.

(4.131)

By (
4.126
) we have

\displaystyle\rho_{r}(C_{1},C_{2},C_{3})=:\sum_{n}\frac{|C_{1}+C_{2}d_{2n}+C_{3}d_{3n}|^{2}}{C_{rn}},

(4.132)

\displaystyle\text{where}\,\,C_{1n}=1\!+\!2D_{n}d_{2n}d_{3n},\,\,C_{2n}=d_{2n}^{2}\!+\!2D_{n}d_{2n}d_{3n},\,\,C_{3n}=d_{3n}^{2}\!+\!2D_{n}d_{2n}d_{3n}.

Consider the
case
(1)=(bbb)
.
We prove the analogue of Lemma
3.27
for the case
m=3
.

Lemma 4.51
.

Let
for all
s=(s_{12},s_{23},s_{13})\in\big(\mathbb{R}_{+}\big)^{3}
holds
(
4.125
).
Then

\Delta(Y_{3}^{(3)},Y_{1}^{(3)},Y_{2}^{(3)})=\Delta(Y_{2}^{(2)},Y_{3}^{(2)},Y_{1}^{(2)})=\Delta(Y_{1}^{(1)},Y_{2}^{(1)},Y_{3}^{(1)})=\infty.

(4.133)

Proof 29
.

For
1\leq r<s\leq 3
set

\displaystyle\frac{b_{sn}}{b_{rn}}\!=\!s^{4}_{rs}(1\!+\!c_{sr,n})\quad\text{with}\quad\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}c^{2}_{sr,n}=\infty,\quad\lim_{n\to\infty}c_{sr,n}=0.

For
b_{1n}\equiv 1
we have

\displaystyle b_{2n}=s^{4}_{12}(1+c_{21,n}),\,\,b_{3n}=s^{4}_{13}(1+c_{31,n}),\,\,

\displaystyle\frac{b_{3n}}{b_{2n}}=\frac{s^{4}_{13}}{s^{4}_{12}}\frac{1+c_{31,n}}{1+c_{21,n}}=s^{4}_{23}(1+c_{32,n}),\quad c_{32,n}=\frac{1+c_{31,n}}{1+c_{21,n}}-1,\,\,s_{23}=\frac{s_{13}}{s_{12}},

\displaystyle\sum_{n}c^{2}_{32,n}\!=\!\sum_{n}\Big(\frac{1+c_{31,n}}{1+c_{21,n}}\!-\!1\Big)^{2}\!\!=\!\sum_{n}\Big(\frac{c_{31,n}\!-\!c_{21,n}}{1+c_{21,n}}\Big)^{2}\!\sim\!\sum_{n}(c_{21,n}\!-\!c_{31,n})^{2}\!=\!\infty.

Finally, we get

\sum_{n}c_{21,n}^{2}=\infty,\quad\sum_{n}c_{31,n}^{2}=\infty,\quad\sum_{n}(c_{21,n}-c_{31,n})^{2}=\infty.

(4.134)

By (
4.131
) and (
4.132
) we get

\displaystyle\rho_{r}(C_{1},C_{2},C_{3})\!=\!\|C_{1}Y_{1}^{(r)}\!+\!C_{2}Y_{2}^{(r)}\!+\!C_{2}Y_{3}^{(r)}\|^{2}\!=\!\sum_{n}\frac{|C_{1}+C_{2}d_{2n}+C_{3}d_{3n}|^{2}}{C_{rn}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{n}\frac{|C_{1}+C_{2}s^{4}_{12}(1+c_{21,n})+C_{3}s^{4}_{13}(1+c_{31,n})|^{2}}{C_{rn}}.

The latter expression is divergent if
C_{1}+C_{2}s^{4}_{12}+C_{3}s^{4}_{13}\not=0
since

\lim_{n\to\infty}c_{21,n}=\lim_{n\to\infty}c_{31,n}=0
and
A_{1}\leq C_{rn}\leq A_{2}
.

In the case when
C_{1}+C_{2}s^{4}_{12}+C_{3}s^{4}_{13}=0
we get

\rho_{r}(C_{1},C_{2},C_{3})=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{|C_{2}s^{4}_{12}c_{21,n}+C_{3}s^{4}_{13}c_{31,n}|^{2}}{C_{rn}}=:\rho_{r}(C_{2},C_{3}).

(4.135)

The latter expression is divergent by the first two relations in (
4.134
) when 1)
C_{2}C_{3}>0
, 2)

C_{2}=0
and
C_{3}\not=0
, 3)
C_{2}\not=0
and
C_{3}=0
.

If
C_{2}C_{3}<0
we have by the last relation in (
4.134
)

\displaystyle\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{|C_{2}s^{4}_{12}c_{21,n}-C_{3}s^{4}_{13}c_{31,n}|^{2}}{C_{rn}}\sim\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}|C_{2}s^{4}_{12}c_{21,n}-C_{3}s^{4}_{13}c_{31,n}|^{2}=\infty,

since
(s_{12},s_{13})=\frac{1}{s_{1}}(s_{2},s_{3})\in(\mathbb{R}^{*})^{2}
are arbitrary.

Consider the
case (2)=(aaa)
.
Now, see (
4.130
), we have

l_{21}\!=\!\lim_{n}\frac{b_{2n}}{b_{1n}}\!=\!\infty,\,\,\,l_{32}\!=\!\lim_{n}\frac{b_{3n}}{b_{2n}}\!=\!\infty,\,\,\,\text{ therefore,}\,\,\,l_{31}\!=\!\lim_{n}\frac{b_{3n}}{b_{1n}}\!=\!\infty.

(4.136)

Since
b_{1n}\equiv 1
we conclude that

l_{21}\!=\!\lim_{n}d_{2n}=\infty\quad\text{and}\quad l_{31}\!=\!\lim_{n}d_{3n}=\infty.

(4.137)

Therefore, we get for some
C>0
and all
n\in\mathbb{Z}

1\leq D_{n}=1+\frac{1}{d_{2n}}+\frac{1}{d_{3n}}\leq C.

(4.138)

By (
4.131
) and (
4.132
)
we obtain

\displaystyle\rho_{r}(C_{1},C_{2},C_{3})\!=\!\|C_{1}Y_{1}^{(r)}\!+\!C_{2}Y_{2}^{(r)}\!+\!C_{2}Y_{3}^{(r)}\|^{2}\!=\!\sum_{n}\frac{|C_{1}+C_{2}d_{2n}+C_{3}d_{3n}|^{2}}{C_{rn}}

\displaystyle\sim\sum_{n}\frac{|C_{1}+C_{2}d_{2n}+C_{3}d_{3n}|^{2}}{C^{\prime}_{rn}}=:\rho^{\prime}_{r}(C_{1},C_{2},C_{3}),

\displaystyle\text{where}\quad C^{\prime}_{rn}=1+2d_{2n}d_{3n},\quad C^{\prime}_{rn}=d^{2}_{2n}+2d_{2n}d_{3n},\quad C^{\prime}_{rn}=d^{2}_{3n}+2d_{2n}d_{3n}.

We should study when
\rho^{\prime}_{r}(C_{1},C_{2},C_{3})=\infty
for some
1\leq r\leq 3
:

\displaystyle\sum_{n}\frac{|C_{1}\!+\!C_{2}d_{2n}\!+\!C_{3}d_{3n}|^{2}}{1+2d_{2n}d_{3n}},\,\,\sum_{n}\frac{|C_{1}\!+\!C_{2}d_{2n}\!+\!C_{3}d_{3n}|^{2}}{d^{2}_{2n}+2d_{2n}d_{3n}},\,\,\sum_{n}\frac{|C_{1}\!+\!C_{2}d_{2n}\!+\!C_{3}d_{3n}|^{2}}{d^{2}_{3n}+2d_{2n}d_{3n}}.

Denoting as before
d_{n}=\frac{d_{3n}}{d_{2n}}=:l^{-1}_{n}
, we get

\displaystyle\rho^{\prime}_{1}(C_{1},C_{2},C_{3})=\sum_{n}\frac{|C_{1}\!+\!C_{2}d_{2n}\!+\!C_{3}d_{3n}|^{2}}{1+2d_{2n}d_{3n}}=\sum_{n}\frac{|\frac{C_{1}}{d_{2n}}\!+\!C_{2}\!+\!C_{3}d_{n}|^{2}}{\frac{1}{d^{2}_{2n}}+2d_{n}}

\displaystyle\sim\sum_{n}\frac{|C_{2}\!+\!C_{3}d_{n}|^{2}}{2d_{n}},\quad\rho^{\prime}_{2}(C_{1},C_{2},C_{3})=\sum_{n}\frac{|C_{1}\!+\!C_{2}d_{2n}\!+\!C_{3}d_{3n}|^{2}}{d^{2}_{2n}+2d_{2n}d_{3n}}=

\displaystyle\sum_{n}\frac{|\frac{C_{1}}{d_{2n}}\!+\!C_{2}\!+\!C_{3}d_{n}|^{2}}{1+2d_{n}}\sim\sum_{n}\frac{|C_{2}\!+\!C_{3}d_{n}|^{2}}{1+2d_{n}},

\displaystyle\rho^{\prime}_{3}(C_{1},C_{2},C_{3})=\sum_{n}\frac{|C_{1}\!+\!C_{2}d_{2n}\!+\!C_{3}d_{3n}|^{2}}{d^{2}_{3n}+2d_{2n}d_{3n}}=

\displaystyle\sum_{n}\frac{|\frac{C_{1}}{d_{2n}}\!+\!C_{2}\!+\!C_{3}d_{n}|^{2}}{d_{n}^{2}+2d_{n}}\sim\sum_{n}\frac{|C_{2}\!+\!C_{3}d_{n}|^{2}}{d_{n}^{2}+2d_{n}}=\sum_{n}\frac{|C_{2}l_{n}\!+\!C_{3}|^{2}}{1+2l_{n}}.

By Lemma
4.36
we get when
C_{2}C_{3}^{-1}>0

\displaystyle\rho^{\prime}_{1}(C_{1},C_{2},-C_{3})\sim\sum_{n}\frac{|C_{2}\!-\!C_{3}d_{n}|^{2}}{2d_{n}}>\sum_{n}\frac{|C_{2}\!-\!C_{3}d_{n}|^{2}}{1+2d_{n}}\sim\sum_{n}c^{2}_{n}\sim\Sigma_{23}(s),

\displaystyle\rho^{\prime}_{2}(C_{1},C_{2},-C_{3})\sim\sum_{n}\frac{|C_{2}\!-\!C_{3}d_{n}|^{2}}{1+2d_{n}}\sim\sum_{n}c^{2}_{n}\sim\Sigma_{23}(s),

(4.139)

\displaystyle\rho^{\prime}_{3}(C_{1},C_{2},-C_{3})\sim\sum_{n}\frac{|C_{2}l_{n}\!-\!C_{3}|^{2}}{1+2l_{n}}\sim\sum_{n}e^{2}_{n}\sim\Sigma_{23}(s),

\displaystyle\text{where}\quad d_{n}=C_{2}C_{3}^{-1}(1+c_{n}),\,\,\,l_{n}=C_{3}C_{2}^{-1}(1+e_{n}),\quad\,s^{4}=C_{2}C_{3}^{-1}>0.

But
\Sigma_{23}(s)=\infty
for all
s>0
therefore, for
C_{2}C^{-1}_{3}>0
we have

\rho_{r}(C_{1},C_{2},-C_{3})\sim\Sigma_{23}(s)=\infty.

(4.140)

If
C_{2}C^{-1}_{3}>0
, by (
4.139
) we get

\displaystyle\sum_{n}\frac{|C_{2}\!+\!C_{3}d_{n}|^{2}}{1+2d_{n}}>\sum_{n}\frac{|C_{2}\!-\!C_{3}d_{n}|^{2}}{1+2d_{n}}\sim\sum_{n}c^{2}_{n}\sim\Sigma_{23}(s)=\infty,

\displaystyle\sum_{n}\frac{|C_{2}\!+\!C_{3}d_{n}|^{2}}{1+2d_{n}}\sum_{n}\frac{|C_{2}\!-\!C_{3}d_{n}|^{2}}{1+2d_{n}}\sim\sum_{n}c^{2}_{n}\sim\Sigma_{23}(s)=\infty,

\displaystyle\sum_{n}\frac{|C_{2}l_{n}\!+\!C_{3}|^{2}}{1+2l_{n}}>\sum_{n}\frac{|C_{2}l_{n}\!-\!C_{3}|^{2}}{1+2l_{n}}\sim\sum_{n}e^{2}_{n}\sim\Sigma_{23}(s)=\infty.

Therefore,

\rho_{r}(C_{1},C_{2},C_{3})\!=\!\infty
for every
(C_{1},C_{2},C_{3})\!\in\!\mathbb{R}^{3}\setminus\!\{0\}
.

Consider the
case (3)=(aba)
.
In this case, see (
4.130
), we have

l_{21}\!=\!\lim_{n}\frac{b_{2n}}{b_{1n}}\!=\!\infty,\quad l_{32}\!=\!\lim_{n}\frac{b_{3n}}{b_{2n}}<\infty,\quad\text{ therefore,}\quad l_{31}\!=\!\lim_{n}\frac{b_{3n}}{b_{1n}}\!=\!\infty.

So, we have again, see (
4.137
)

l_{21}\!=\!\lim_{n}d_{2n}=\infty,\quad\text{and}\quad l_{31}\!=\!\lim_{n}d_{3n}=\infty.

We are reduced to the case (2).

Consider the
case (4)=(baa).

Now, see (
4.130
), we have

l_{21}\!=\!\lim_{n}d_{2n}<\infty,\quad\text{and}\quad l_{31}\!=\!\lim_{n}d_{3n}=\infty.

Hence (
4.138
) holds too and we can use
all estimations of the case (1).
∎

Remark 4.18
.

In the cases (1)–(4) by Lemma
4.51
and Lemmas
5.52
–
5.54
we can approximate
x_{rn}
for all
1\leq r\leq 3
and
n\in\mathbb{Z}
and the irreducibility is proved.

5
Approximation of
D_{kn}
and
x_{kn}

5.1
Approximation of

x_{kn}
by
A_{nk}A_{tk}

For
m=3
, consider three rows as follows

\left(\begin{array}[]{cccccc}...&b_{11}&b_{12}&...&b_{1n}&...\\
...&b_{21}&b_{22}&...&b_{2n}&...\\
...&b_{31}&b_{32}&...&b_{3n}&...\end{array}\right).

Set

\lambda_{k}^{(r)}=(b_{1k}+b_{2k}+b_{3k})^{2}-(b_{1k}^{2}+b_{2k}^{2}+b_{3k}^{2}-b_{rk}^{2}),\,\,r=1,2,3,\,\,k\in\mathbb{Z}.

(5.1)

Denote by
Y_{r}^{(s)}
the following vectors:

x_{rk}^{(s)}=b_{rk}/\sqrt{\lambda_{k}^{(s)}},\,\,k\in{\mathbb{Z}},\quad Y_{r}^{(s)}=(x_{rk}^{(s)})_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}.

(5.2)

Lemma 5.52
.

For any
n,t\in{\mathbb{Z}}
one has

x_{1n}x_{1t}{\bf 1}\in\langle A_{nk}A_{tk}{\bf 1}\mid k\in{\mathbb{Z}}\rangle\,\,\Leftrightarrow\,\,\Delta(Y_{1}^{(1)},Y_{2}^{(1)},Y_{3}^{(1)})=\infty.

Similarly, using the cyclic permutation of vectors, and changing
\lambda_{k}^{(r)}
we arrive at the following lemma.

Lemma 5.53
.

For any
n,t\in{\mathbb{Z}}
one has

x_{2n}x_{2t}{\bf 1}\in\langle A_{nk}A_{tk}{\bf 1}\mid k\in{\mathbb{Z}}\rangle\,\,\Leftrightarrow\,\,\Delta(Y_{2}^{(2)},Y_{3}^{(2)},Y_{1}^{(2)}))=\infty.

Lemma 5.54
.

For any
n,t\in{\mathbb{Z}}
one has

x_{3n}x_{3t}{\bf 1}\in\langle A_{nk}A_{tk}{\bf 1}\mid k\in{\mathbb{Z}}\rangle\,\,\Leftrightarrow\,\,\Delta(Y_{3}^{(3)},Y_{1}^{(3)},Y_{2}^{(3)})=\infty.

Proof 30
.

The proof of Lemma
5.52
is also based on Lemma
7.72
.
We study when

x_{1n}x_{1t}{\bf 1}\in\langle A_{nk}A_{tk}{\bf 1}\mid k\in{\mathbb{Z}}\rangle.

Since

\displaystyle A_{nk}A_{tk}=(x_{1n}D_{1k}+x_{2n}D_{2k}+x_{3n}D_{3k})(x_{1t}D_{1k}+x_{2t}D_{2k}+x_{3t}D_{3k})

\displaystyle=x_{1n}x_{1t}D_{1k}^{2}+x_{2n}x_{2t}D_{2k}^{2}+x_{3n}x_{3t}D_{3k}^{2}+(x_{1n}x_{2t}+x_{2n}x_{1t})D_{1k}D_{2k}+

\displaystyle(x_{1n}x_{3t}+x_{3n}x_{1t})D_{1k}D_{3k}+(x_{2n}x_{3t}+x_{3n}x_{2t})D_{2k}D_{3k}

and
MD_{1k}^{2}{\bf 1}=-\frac{b_{1k}}{2}
, we take
t=(t_{k})
as follows:

-\sum_{k=-m}^{m}t_{k}\frac{b_{1k}}{2}=(t,b^{\prime})=1
, where
t=(t_{k})_{k=-m}^{m}
and
b^{\prime}=-(\frac{b_{1k}}{2})_{k=-m}^{m}\sim b=(b_{1k})_{k=-m}^{m}
.
We have

\displaystyle\|\big[\sum_{k=-m}^{m}t_{k}A_{nk}A_{tk}-x_{1n}x_{1t}\big]{\bf 1}\|^{2}=

\displaystyle\|\sum_{k=-m}^{m}t_{k}\big[x_{1n}x_{1t}\Big(D_{1k}^{2}\!+\!\frac{b_{1k}}{2}\Big)\!+\!x_{2n}x_{2t}D_{2k}^{2}\!+\!x_{3n}x_{3t}D_{3k}^{2}\!+\!(x_{1n}x_{2t}+x_{2n}x_{1t})\times

\displaystyle D_{1k}D_{2k}+(x_{1n}x_{3t}+x_{3n}x_{1t})D_{1k}D_{3k}+(x_{2n}x_{3t}+x_{3n}x_{2t})D_{2k}D_{3k}\Big]{\bf 1}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{-m\leq k,r\leq m}(f_{k},f_{r})t_{k}t_{r}=:(A_{2m+1}t,t),

where
A_{2m+1}=\big((f_{k},f_{r})\big)_{k,r=-m}^{m}
and

f_{k}=\sum_{r=1}^{3}f_{k}^{r}+\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq 3}f_{k}^{ij},

f_{k}^{r}=x_{rn}x_{rt}\Big(D_{rk}^{2}+\frac{b_{rk}}{2}\delta_{1r}\Big){\bf 1},\quad f_{k}^{ij}=(x_{in}x_{jt}+x_{jn}x_{it})D_{ik}D_{jk}{\bf 1}

(5.3)

for
1\leq r\leq 3,\,\,1\leq i<j\leq 3
.
Since

f_{k}^{r}\perp f_{k}^{ij},\quad f_{k}^{ij}\perp f_{k}^{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}}

for different
(ij),\,\,(i^{\prime}j^{\prime})
,
writing
c_{kn}=\|x_{kn}\|^{2}=\frac{1}{2b_{kn}}+a_{kn}^{2}
, we get

\displaystyle(f_{k},f_{k})=\sum_{r=1}^{3}\|f_{k}^{r}\|^{2}+\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq 3}\|f_{k}^{ij}\|^{2}=

\displaystyle c_{1n}c_{1t}2\Big(\frac{b_{1k}}{2}\Big)^{2}+c_{2n}c_{2t}3\Big(\frac{b_{2k}}{2}\Big)^{2}+c_{3n}c_{3t}3\Big(\frac{b_{3k}}{2}\Big)^{2}+

\displaystyle\big(c_{1n}c_{2t}+c_{1t}c_{2n}+2a_{1n}a_{2t}a_{1t}a_{2n}\big)\frac{b_{1k}}{2}\frac{b_{2k}}{2}+\big(c_{1n}c_{3t}+c_{3t}c_{1n}+2a_{1n}a_{3t}a_{3t}a_{1n}\big)\times

\displaystyle\frac{b_{1k}}{2}\frac{b_{3k}}{2}+\big(c_{2n}c_{3t}+c_{3t}c_{2n}+2a_{2n}a_{3t}a_{3t}a_{2n}\big)\frac{b_{2k}}{2}\frac{b_{3k}}{2}\sim(b_{1k}+b_{2k}+b_{3k})^{2},

\displaystyle(f_{k},f_{r})=(f_{k}^{2},f_{r}^{2})+(f_{k}^{3},f_{r}^{3})=c_{2n}c_{2t}\frac{b_{2k}}{2}\frac{b_{2r}}{2}+c_{3n}c_{3t}\frac{b_{3k}}{2}\frac{b_{3r}}{2}\sim b_{2k}b_{2r}+b_{3k}b_{3r}.

Finally, we have

(f_{k},f_{k})\sim(b_{1k}+b_{2k}+b_{3k})^{2},\quad(f_{k},f_{r})\sim b_{2k}b_{2r}+b_{3k}b_{3r},\quad k\not=r.

(5.4)

Set

\lambda_{k}=(b_{1k}+b_{2k}+b_{3k})^{2}-(b_{2k}^{2}+b_{3k}^{2}),\quad g_{k}=(b_{2k},b_{3k}),

(5.5)

then

(f_{k},f_{k})\sim\lambda_{k}+(g_{k},g_{k}),\quad(f_{k},f_{r})\sim(g_{k},r_{r}).

(5.6)

For
A_{2m+1}=\big((f_{k},f_{r})\big)_{k,r=-m}^{m},
and

b=-(b_{1k}/2)_{k=-m}^{m}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2m+1}
we have

A_{2m+1}=\sum_{k=-m}^{m}\lambda_{k}E_{kk}+\gamma(g_{-m},\dots,g_{0},\dots,g_{m}).

To finish the proof, it suffices to invoke Lemma
7.72
.
∎

5.2
Approximation of
D_{rn}
by
A_{kn}

We will formulate several lemmas, which will be useful for
approximation of the independent variables
x_{kn}
and operators

D_{kn}
by combinations of the generators
A_{kn}
.
The generators
A_{kn}
have the following form:

A_{kn}=x_{1k}D_{1n}+x_{2k}D_{2n}+x_{3k}D_{3n},\,\,k,n\in\mathbb{Z}.

For
m=3
, consider three rows as follows

\left(\begin{array}[]{cccccc}...&a_{11}&a_{12}&...&a_{1n}&...\\
...&a_{21}&a_{22}&...&a_{2n}&...\\
...&a_{31}&a_{32}&...&a_{2n}&...\end{array}\right).

Set

\lambda_{k}=\frac{1}{2b_{1k}}+\frac{1}{2b_{2k}}+\frac{1}{2b_{3k}}.

(5.7)

Denote by
Y_{1},Y_{2}
and
Y_{3}
the three following vectors:

x_{rk}=\frac{a_{rk}}{\sqrt{\lambda_{k}}},\,\,k\in{\mathbb{Z}},\quad Y_{r}=(x_{rk})_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}.

(5.8)

The proofs of Lemmas
5.55
–
5.57
and
5.52
–
5.54

are based on Lemma
7.72
.

Lemma 5.55
.

For any
l\in{\mathbb{Z}}
we have

D_{1l}{\bf 1}\in\langle A_{kl}{\bf 1}\mid k\in{\mathbb{Z}}\rangle\quad\Leftrightarrow\quad\Delta(Y_{1},Y_{2},Y_{3})=\infty.

Similarly, by cyclic permutation
of the vectors,
we obtain the following two lemmas.

Lemma 5.56
.

For any
l\in{\mathbb{Z}}
we have

D_{2l}{\bf 1}\in\langle A_{kl}{\bf 1}\mid k\in{\mathbb{Z}}\rangle\quad\Leftrightarrow\quad\Delta(Y_{2},Y_{3},Y_{1})=\infty.

Lemma 5.57
.

For any
l\in{\mathbb{Z}}
we have

D_{3l}{\bf 1}\in\langle A_{kl}{\bf 1}\mid k\in{\mathbb{Z}}\rangle\quad\Leftrightarrow\quad\Delta(Y_{3},Y_{1},Y_{2})=\infty.

Proof 31
.

We determine when the inclusion

D_{1n}{\bf 1}\in\langle A_{kn}{\bf 1}=(x_{1k}D_{1n}+x_{2k}D_{2n}+x_{3k}D_{3n}){\bf 1}\mid k\in{\mathbb{Z}}\rangle

holds. Fix
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
, since
Mx_{1k}=a_{1k}
, we put

\sum_{k=-m}^{m}t_{k}a_{1k}=(t,b)=1,

where
t=(t_{k})_{k=-m}^{m}
and
b=(a_{1k})_{k=-m}^{m}
. We have

\displaystyle\|\big[\sum_{k=-m}^{m}t_{k}(x_{1k}D_{1n}+x_{2k}D_{2n}+x_{3k}D_{3n})-D_{1n}\big]{\bf 1}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|\sum_{k=-m}^{m}t_{k}[(x_{1k}-a_{1k})D_{1n}+x_{2k}D_{2n}+x_{3k}D_{3n}]{\bf 1}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{-m\leq k,r\leq m}(f_{k},f_{r})t_{k}t_{r}=:(A_{2m+1}t,t),\,\,\text{where}\,\,A_{2m+1}=\big((f_{k},f_{r})\big)_{k,r=-m}^{m},

\displaystyle\quad\text{ and}\quad f_{k}=[(x_{1k}-a_{1k})D_{1n}+x_{2k}D_{2n}+x_{3k}D_{3n}]{\bf 1}.

We get

\displaystyle(f_{k},f_{k})=\|\left[(x_{1k}-a_{1k})D_{1n}+x_{2k}D_{2n}+x_{3k}D_{3n}\right]{\bf 1}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2b_{1k}}\frac{b_{1n}}{2}+\Big(\frac{1}{2b_{2k}}+a_{2k}^{2}\Big)\frac{b_{2n}}{2}+\Big(\frac{1}{2b_{3k}}+a_{3k}^{2}\Big)\frac{b_{3n}}{2}

\displaystyle\sim\frac{1}{2b_{1k}}+\frac{1}{2b_{2k}}+\frac{1}{2b_{3k}}+a_{2k}^{2}+a_{3k}^{2},

\displaystyle(f_{k},f_{r})=\Big(\big[(x_{1k}-a_{1k})D_{1n}+x_{2k}D_{2n}+x_{3k}D_{3n}\big]{\bf 1},

\displaystyle\big[(x_{1r}-a_{1r})D_{1n}+x_{2r}D_{2n}+x_{3r}D_{3n}\big]{\bf 1}\Big)

\displaystyle=(x_{2k}{\bf 1},x_{2r}{\bf 1})(D_{2n}{\bf 1},D_{2n}{\bf 1})+(x_{3k}{\bf 1},x_{3r}{\bf 1})(D_{3n}{\bf 1},D_{3n}{\bf 1})

\displaystyle=a_{2k}a_{2r}\frac{b_{2n}}{2}+a_{3k}a_{3r}\frac{b_{3n}}{2}\sim a_{2k}a_{2r}+a_{3k}a_{3r}.

Finally, we have

(f_{k},f_{k})\!\sim\!\frac{1}{2b_{1k}}+\frac{1}{2b_{2k}}+\frac{1}{2b_{3k}}+a_{2k}^{2}+a_{3k}^{2},\,\,\,\,\,(f_{k},f_{r})\sim a_{2k}a_{2r}+a_{3k}a_{3r},\,\,k\not=r.

(5.9)

If we denote

\lambda_{k}=\frac{1}{2b_{1k}}+\frac{1}{2b_{2k}}+\frac{1}{2b_{3k}},\quad g_{k}=(a_{2k},a_{3k}),

(5.10)

then we have

(f_{k},f_{k})\sim\lambda_{k}+(g_{k},g_{k}),\quad(f_{k},f_{r})\sim(g_{k},g_{r}).

(5.11)

For
A_{2m+1}=\big((f_{k},f_{r})\big)_{k,r=-m}^{m},
and

b=(a_{1k})_{k=-m}^{m}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2m+1}
we have

A_{2m+1}=\sum_{k=-m}^{m}\lambda_{k}E_{kk}+\gamma(g_{-m},\dots,g_{0},\dots,g_{m}).

To finish the proof, it suffices to apply Lemma
7.72
.
∎

The proofs of Lemmas
5.56
and
5.57
are exactly the same.

5.3
Approximation of
x_{rk}
by
D_{rn}A_{kn}

Lemma 5.58
.

For any
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
we get

x_{1k}{\bf 1}\in\langle D_{1n}A_{kn}{\bf 1}\mid n\in{\mathbb{Z}}\rangle\quad\Leftrightarrow\quad\sum_{n\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\frac{b_{1n}}{b_{1n}+b_{2n}+b_{3n}}=\infty.

Proof 32
.

Since

D_{1n}A_{kn}=x_{1k}D_{1n}^{2}+x_{2k}D_{1n}D_{2n}+x_{3k}D_{1n}D_{3n}

and
MD_{1k}^{2}{\bf 1}\!=\!-\!\frac{b_{1k}}{2}
, we take
t\!=\!(t_{k})_{k=-m}^{m}
as follows:

(t,b^{\prime})=1
, where
t=(t_{k})_{k=-m}^{m}
and
b^{\prime}=-(\frac{b_{1k}}{2})_{k=-m}^{m}\sim b=-(b_{1k})_{k=-m}^{m}
.
We have

\displaystyle\|\big[\sum_{n=-m}^{m}t_{n}D_{1n}A_{kn}-x_{1k}\big]{\bf 1}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|\sum_{n=-m}^{m}t_{n}\left[x_{1k}\Big(D_{1n}^{2}+\frac{b_{1n}}{2}\Big)+x_{2k}D_{1n}D_{2n}+x_{3k}D_{1n}D_{3n}\right]{\bf 1}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{-m\leq n,r\leq m}(f_{n},f_{r})t_{k}t_{r}=:(A_{2m+1}t,t),

where
A_{2m+1}=\big((f_{n},f_{r})\big)_{n,r=-m}^{m}

and

f_{n}=\left[x_{1k}\Big(D_{1n}^{2}+\frac{b_{1n}}{2}\Big)+x_{2k}D_{1n}D_{2n}+x_{3k}D_{1n}D_{3n}\right]{\bf 1}.

We have

(f_{n},f_{n})\sim b_{1k}(b_{1n}+b_{2n}+b_{3n})\quad\text{and}\quad(f_{n},f_{k})=0\quad\text{for}\quad n\not=k.

Therefore, by (
6.3
)

\min_{t\in\mathbb{R}^{2m+1}}\|\left[\sum_{n=-m}^{m}t_{n}D_{1n}A_{kn}-x_{1k}\right]{\bf 1}\|^{2}=\left(\sum_{n=-m}^{m}\frac{b_{1k}}{b_{1n}+b_{2n}+b_{3n}}\right)^{-1}.\hskip 28.45274pt\Box

Lemma 5.59
.

For any
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
we have

x_{2k}{\bf 1}\in\langle D_{2n}A_{kn}{\bf 1}\mid n\in{\mathbb{Z}}\rangle\quad\Leftrightarrow\quad\sum_{n\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\frac{b_{2n}}{b_{1n}+b_{2n}+b_{3n}}=\infty.

Lemma 5.60
.

For any
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
we have

x_{3k}{\bf 1}\in\langle D_{3n}A_{kn}{\bf 1}\mid n\in{\mathbb{Z}}\rangle\quad\Leftrightarrow\quad\sum_{n\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\frac{b_{3n}}{b_{1n}+b_{2n}+b_{3n}}=\infty.

5.4
Approximation of
D_{kn}
by
x_{rk}A_{kn}

Set

\displaystyle\lambda_{k}^{(1)}\!=\!\Big(\frac{1}{2b_{1k}}\!+\!a_{1k}^{2}\Big)\Big(\frac{1}{2b_{1k}}\!+\!\frac{1}{2b_{2k}}\!+\!\frac{1}{2b_{3k}}\!+\!a_{2k}^{2}+a_{3k}^{2}\Big)\!-\!a_{1k}^{2}(a_{2k}^{2}\!+\!a_{3k}^{2}),

(5.12)

\displaystyle Y_{11}\!=\!\left(\frac{\frac{1}{2b_{1k}}\!+\!a_{1k}^{2}}{\sqrt{\lambda_{k}^{(1)}}}\right)_{k\in\mathbb{Z}},Y_{12}\!=\!\left(\frac{a_{1k}a_{2k}}{\sqrt{\lambda_{k}^{(1)}}}\right)_{k\in\mathbb{Z}},Y_{13}\!=\!\left(\frac{a_{1k}a_{3k}}{\sqrt{\lambda_{k}^{(1)}}}\right)_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}.

(5.13)

Lemma 5.61
.

For any
n\in{\mathbb{Z}}
we have

D_{1n}{\bf 1}\in\langle x_{1k}A_{kn}{\bf 1}\mid k\in{\mathbb{Z}}\rangle\quad\Leftrightarrow\quad\Delta(Y_{11},Y_{12},Y_{13})=\infty.

Proof 33
.

We determine when the inclusion

D_{1n}{\bf 1}\in\langle x_{1k}A_{kn}{\bf 1}=(x^{2}_{1k}D_{1n}+x_{1k}x_{2k}D_{2n}+x_{1k}x_{3k}D_{3n}){\bf 1}\mid k\in{\mathbb{Z}}\rangle

holds. Fix
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
, since
Mx^{2}_{1k}=\frac{1}{2b_{1k}}+a^{2}_{1k}
, we put

\sum_{k=-m}^{m}t_{k}\big(\frac{1}{2b_{1k}}+a^{2}_{1k}\big)=(t,b)=1,

where
t=(t_{k})_{k=-m}^{m}
and
b=(\frac{1}{2b_{1k}}+a^{2}_{1k})_{k=-m}^{m}
. We have

\displaystyle\|\big[\sum_{k=-m}^{m}t_{k}(x^{2}_{1k}D_{1n}+x_{1k}x_{2k}D_{2n}+x_{1k}x_{3k}D_{3n})-D_{1n}\big]{\bf 1}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|\sum_{k=-m}^{m}t_{k}\Big[\Big(x^{2}_{1k}-\Big(\frac{1}{2b_{1k}}+a^{2}_{1k}\Big)\Big)D_{1n}+x_{1k}x_{2k}D_{2n}+x_{1k}x_{3k}D_{3n}\Big]{\bf 1}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{-m\leq k,r\leq m}(f_{k},f_{r})t_{k}t_{r}=:(A_{2m+1}t,t),\,\,\text{where}\,\,A_{2m+1}=\big((f_{k},f_{r})\big)_{k,r=-m}^{m},

\displaystyle\quad\text{ and}\quad f_{k}=\Big[\Big(x^{2}_{1k}-\Big(\frac{1}{2b_{1k}}+a^{2}_{1k}\Big)\Big)D_{1n}+x_{1k}x_{2k}D_{2n}+x_{1k}x_{3k}D_{3n}\Big]{\bf 1}.

Since
M|\psi-M|\psi||^{2}=M\psi^{2}-|M\psi|^{2}
we have

\displaystyle M\left|x^{2}_{1k}-\Big(\frac{1}{2b_{1k}}+a^{2}_{1k}\Big)\right|^{2}=Mx^{4}_{1k}-\Big(\frac{1}{2b_{1k}}+a^{2}_{1k}\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{3}{(2b_{1k})^{2}}+6\frac{1}{2b_{1k}}a_{1k}^{2}+a^{4}_{1k}-\Big(\frac{1}{2b_{1k}}+a^{2}_{1k}\Big)^{2}=\frac{1}{2b_{1k}}\Big(\frac{2}{2b_{1k}}+4a^{2}_{1k}\Big),

we get

\displaystyle(f_{k},f_{k})=\|\Big[\Big(x^{2}_{1k}-\Big(\frac{1}{2b_{1k}}+a^{2}_{1k}\Big)\Big)D_{1n}+x_{1k}x_{2k}D_{2n}+x_{1k}x_{3k}D_{3n}\Big]{\bf 1}\|^{2}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2b_{1k}}\Big(\frac{2}{2b_{1k}}\!+\!4a^{2}_{1k}\Big)\frac{b_{1n}}{2}\!+\!\Big(\frac{1}{2b_{1k}}+a_{1k}^{2}\Big)\Big(\frac{1}{2b_{2k}}+a_{2k}^{2}\Big)\frac{b_{2n}}{2}\!+\!\Big(\frac{1}{2b_{1k}}+a_{1k}^{2}\Big)\frac{b_{3n}}{2}

\displaystyle\times\Big(\frac{1}{2b_{3k}}+a_{3k}^{2}\Big)\sim\Big(\frac{1}{2b_{1k}}+a_{1k}^{2}\Big)\Big(\frac{1}{2b_{1k}}+\frac{1}{2b_{2k}}+\frac{1}{2b_{3k}}+a_{2k}^{2}+a_{3k}^{2}\Big)\,

\displaystyle(f_{k},f_{r})=\Big(\Big[\Big(x^{2}_{1k}-\Big(\frac{1}{2b_{1k}}+a^{2}_{1k}\Big)\Big)D_{1n}+x_{1k}x_{2k}D_{2n}+x_{1k}x_{3k}D_{3n}\Big]{\bf 1},

\displaystyle\Big[\Big(x^{2}_{1r}-\Big(\frac{1}{2b_{1r}}+a^{2}_{1r}\Big)\Big)D_{1n}+x_{1r}x_{2r}D_{2n}+x_{1r}x_{3r}D_{3n}\Big]{\bf 1}\Big)=

\displaystyle(x_{1k}{\bf 1},x_{1r}{\bf 1})(x_{2k}{\bf 1},x_{2r}{\bf 1})(D_{2n}{\bf 1},D_{2n}{\bf 1})\!+\!(x_{1k}{\bf 1},x_{1r}{\bf 1})(x_{3k}{\bf 1},x_{3r}{\bf 1})(D_{3n}{\bf 1},D_{3n}{\bf 1})

\displaystyle=a_{1k}a_{1r}a_{2k}a_{2r}\frac{b_{2n}}{2}+a_{1k}a_{1r}a_{3k}a_{3r}\frac{b_{3n}}{2}\simeq a_{1k}a_{1r}(a_{2k}a_{2r}+a_{3k}a_{3r}).

Finally, we have

\displaystyle(f_{k},f_{k})\!\sim\!\Big(\frac{1}{2b_{1k}}+a_{1k}^{2}\Big)\Big(\frac{1}{2b_{1k}}+\frac{1}{2b_{2k}}+\frac{1}{2b_{3k}}+a_{2k}^{2}+a_{3k}^{2}\Big),

(5.14)

\displaystyle(f_{k},f_{r})\sim a_{1k}a_{1r}(a_{2k}a_{2r}+a_{3k}a_{3r}),\,\,k\not=r.

Set

\displaystyle\lambda_{k}^{(1)}=\Big(\frac{1}{2b_{1k}}+a_{1k}^{2}\Big)\Big(\frac{1}{2b_{1k}}+\frac{1}{2b_{2k}}+\frac{1}{2b_{3k}}+a_{2k}^{2}+a_{3k}^{2}\Big)-a_{1k}^{2}(a_{2k}^{2}+a_{3k}^{2}),

\displaystyle g_{k}=a_{1k}(a_{2k},a_{3k}),

(5.15)

then

(f_{k},f_{k})=\lambda_{k}^{(1)}+(g_{k},g_{k})\quad(f_{k},f_{r})\sim(g_{k},g_{r}),\,\,k\not=r.

(5.16)

For
A_{2m+1}=\big((f_{k},f_{r})\big)_{k,r=-m}^{m},
and

b=(a_{1k})_{k=-m}^{m}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2m+1}
we have

A_{2m+1}=\sum_{k=-m}^{m}\lambda_{k}E_{kk}+\gamma(g_{-m},\dots,g_{0},\dots,g_{m}).

To finish the proof, it suffices to apply Lemma
7.72
.
∎

Similarly, by cyclic permutation of the indices, we obtain the following lemmas.

Lemma 5.62
.

For any
l\in{\mathbb{Z}}
we have

D_{2l}{\bf 1}\in\langle x_{2k}A_{kl}{\bf 1}\mid k\in{\mathbb{Z}}\rangle\quad\Leftrightarrow\quad\Delta(Y_{22},Y_{23},Y_{21})=\infty.

Lemma 5.63
.

For any
l\in{\mathbb{Z}}
we have

D_{3l}{\bf 1}\in\langle x_{3k}A_{kl}{\bf 1}\mid k\in{\mathbb{Z}}\rangle\quad\Leftrightarrow\quad\Delta(Y_{33},Y_{31},Y_{32})=\infty.

Here we set

\displaystyle\!\!\lambda_{k}^{(2)}\!=\!\Big(\frac{1}{2b_{2k}}\!+\!a_{2k}^{2}\Big)\Big(\frac{1}{2b_{1k}}\!+\!\frac{1}{2b_{2k}}\!+\!\frac{1}{2b_{3k}}+a_{1k}^{2}\!+\!a_{3k}^{2}\Big)\!-\!a_{2k}^{2}(a_{1k}^{2}\!+\!a_{3k}^{2}),\,\,

(5.17)

\displaystyle Y_{21}\!=\!\left(\frac{a_{1k}a_{2k}}{\sqrt{\lambda_{k}^{(2)}}}\right)_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\!\!\!\!,Y_{22}\!=\!\left(\frac{\frac{1}{2b_{2k}}+a_{2k}^{2}}{\sqrt{\lambda_{k}^{(2)}}}\right)_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\!\!\!,Y_{23}\!=\!\left(\frac{a_{2k}a_{3k}}{\sqrt{\lambda_{k}^{(2)}}}\right)_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\!\!\!,

(5.18)

\displaystyle\lambda_{k}^{(3)}\!=\!\Big(\frac{1}{2b_{3k}}\!+\!a_{3k}^{2}\Big)\Big(\frac{1}{2b_{1k}}\!+\!\frac{1}{2b_{2k}}\!+\!\frac{1}{2b_{3k}}\!+\!a_{1k}^{2}\!+\!a_{2k}^{2}\Big)\!-\!a_{3k}^{2}(a_{1k}^{2}\!+\!a_{2k}^{2}),\qquad

(5.19)

\displaystyle Y_{31}=\left(\frac{a_{1k}a_{3k}}{\sqrt{\lambda_{k}^{(3)}}}\right)_{k\in\mathbb{Z}},Y_{32}=\left(\frac{a_{2k}a_{3k}}{\sqrt{\lambda_{k}^{(3)}}}\right)_{k\in\mathbb{Z}},Y_{33}=\left(\frac{\frac{1}{2b_{3k}}+a_{3k}^{2}}{\sqrt{\lambda_{k}^{(3)}}}\right)_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}.

(5.20)

5.5
Approximation of
D_{rn}
by
(x_{3k}-a_{3k})A_{kn}
and
\exp(is_{k}x_{rk})A_{kn}

Lemma 5.64
.

For any
n\in{\mathbb{Z}}
we have

\displaystyle D_{3n}{\bf 1}\in\langle(x_{3k}-a_{3k})A_{kn}{\bf 1}\mid k\in{\mathbb{Z}}\rangle\,\,\,\Leftrightarrow\,\,\,

\displaystyle\Sigma_{3}(\mu):=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{1}{2b_{3k}}\Big(\frac{1}{2b_{1k}}+\frac{1}{2b_{2k}}+\frac{1}{2b_{3k}}+a_{1k}^{2}+a_{2k}^{2}+a_{3k}^{2}\Big)^{-1}\!\!=\!\infty.\qquad

(5.21)

Proof 34
.

We determine when the following inclusion holds

\displaystyle D_{3n}{\bf 1}\in\langle(x_{3k}-a_{3k})A_{kn}{\bf 1}

\displaystyle=\big(x_{1k}(x_{3k}-a_{3k})D_{1n}+x_{2k}(x_{3k}-a_{3k})D_{2n}+x_{3k}(x_{3k}-a_{3k})D_{3n}\big){\bf 1}\mid k\in{\mathbb{Z}}\rangle.

Set
\xi_{3k}=x_{3k}(x_{3k}-a_{3k})
. Fix
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
. We have

M\xi_{3k}{\bf 1}=M(x_{3k}-a_{3k})^{2}{\bf 1}=\frac{1}{2b_{3k}},\quad\text{chose}\quad(t_{k})\,\,\text{as}\,\,\sum_{k=-m}^{m}t_{k}\frac{1}{2b_{3k}}=(t,b)=1,

where we denote
t=(t_{k})_{k=-m}^{m}
and
b=(\frac{1}{2b_{3k}})_{k=-m}^{m}
. We have

\displaystyle\|\big[\sum_{k=-m}^{m}t_{k}\big(x_{1k}(x_{3k}-a_{3k})D_{1n}+x_{2k}(x_{3k}-a_{3k})D_{2n}+\xi_{3k}D_{3n}\big)-D_{3n}\big]{\bf 1}\|^{2}

(5.22)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|\sum_{k=-m}^{m}t_{k}\Big[x_{1k}(x_{3k}-a_{3k})D_{1n}+x_{2k}(x_{3k}-a_{3k})D_{2n}+\big(\xi_{3k}-M\xi_{3k}\big)D_{3n}\Big]{\bf 1}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{-m\leq k,r\leq m}(f_{k},f_{r})t_{k}t_{r}=\sum_{-m\leq k\leq m}(f_{k},f_{k})t_{k}^{2},\,\,\,\text{since}\quad f_{k}\perp f_{r},\quad\text{where}

\displaystyle f_{k}=\Big[x_{1k}(x_{3k}-a_{3k})D_{1n}+x_{2k}(x_{3k}-a_{3k})D_{2n}+\big(\xi_{3k}-M\xi_{3k}\big)D_{3n}\Big]{\bf 1}.

To calculate
M|\xi_{3k}-M\xi_{3k}|^{2}
set

d\mu_{(b,a)}(x)=\sqrt{\frac{b}{\pi}}\exp(-b(x-a)^{2})dx\quad\text{and}\quad d\mu_{(b,0)}(x)=\sqrt{\frac{b}{\pi}}\exp(-bx^{2})dx,

(5.23)

then we get
M\xi_{3k}^{2}=\frac{3}{(2b_{3k})^{2}}+\frac{a_{3k}^{2}}{2b_{3k}}
. Indeed,

\displaystyle Mx^{2}(x-a)^{2}=\int x^{2}(x-a)^{2}d\mu_{(b,a)}(x)=\int x^{2}(x+a)^{2}d\mu_{(b,0)}(x)

\displaystyle=\int(x^{4}+2ax^{3}+a^{2}x^{2})d\mu_{(b,0)}(x)=\frac{3}{(2b)^{2}}+\frac{a^{2}}{2b}.

Since
M|\xi-M|\xi||^{2}=M\xi^{2}-|M\xi|^{2}

we get

M\xi_{3k}^{2}-M|\xi_{3k}|^{2}=\frac{3}{(2b_{3k})^{2}}+\frac{a_{3k}^{2}}{2b_{3k}}-\frac{1}{(2b_{3k})^{2}}=\frac{1}{2b_{3k}}\Big(\frac{2}{2b_{3k}}+a_{3k}^{2}\Big).

Set
f_{sk}=x_{sk}(x_{3k}\!-\!a_{3k})D_{1n}{\bf 1},\,\,1\leq s\leq 2
and
f_{3k}=\big(\xi_{3k}\!-\!M\xi_{3k}\big)D_{3n}{\bf 1}
, then

\displaystyle(f_{k},f_{k})\!=\!\|\Big[x_{1k}(x_{3k}\!-\!a_{3k})D_{1n}\!+\!x_{2k}(x_{3k}\!-\!a_{3k})D_{2n}\!+\!\big(\xi_{3k}\!-\!M\xi_{3k}\big)D_{3n}\Big]{\bf 1}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\Big(\frac{1}{2b_{1k}}+a^{2}_{1k}\Big)\frac{1}{2b_{3k}}\frac{b_{1n}}{2}\!+\!\Big(\frac{1}{2b_{2k}}+a^{2}_{2k}\Big)\frac{1}{2b_{3k}}\frac{b_{2n}}{2}\!+\!\Big(\frac{2}{2b_{3k}}+a^{2}_{3k}\Big)\frac{1}{2b_{3k}}\frac{b_{3n}}{2}

\displaystyle\sim\Big(\frac{1}{2b_{1k}}+\frac{1}{2b_{2k}}+\frac{1}{2b_{3k}}+a_{1k}^{2}+a_{2k}^{2}+a_{3k}^{2}\Big)\frac{1}{2b_{3k}},\,\,\text{since}\,\,f_{lk}\perp f_{sk},\,\,l\not=s,

\displaystyle(f_{k},f_{r})\!=\!\Big(\Big[x_{1k}(x_{3k}\!-\!a_{3k})D_{1n}\!+\!x_{2k}(x_{3k}\!-\!a_{3k})D_{2n}\!+\!\big(\xi_{3k}\!-\!M\xi_{3k}\big)D_{3n}\Big]{\bf 1},

\displaystyle\Big[x_{1r}(x_{3r}-a_{3r})D_{1n}+x_{2r}(x_{3r}-a_{3r})D_{2n}+\big(\xi_{3r}-M\xi_{3r}\big)D_{3n}\Big]{\bf 1}\Big)=0.

The previous equality holds since
f_{lr}\perp f_{sk}
for
1\leq l,s\leq 3
and
r\!\not=\!k
. For

l\not=s
this follows from

(D_{ln}{\bf 1},D_{sn}{\bf 1})\!=\!0
. For
l\!=\!s
it follows from the equalities:

(x_{sk}{\bf 1},x_{sr}{\bf 1})=a_{sk}a_{sr},\,\,1\leq s\leq 2,\quad\Big((\xi_{3k}-M\xi_{3k}){\bf 1},(\xi_{3r}-M\xi_{3r}){\bf 1}\Big)=0.

and
\big((x_{3k}-a_{3k}){\bf 1},(x_{3r}-a_{3r}){\bf 1}\big)=0
. Finally, we have

(f_{k},f_{k})\!\sim\!\Big(\frac{1}{2b_{1k}}+\frac{1}{2b_{2k}}+\frac{1}{2b_{3k}}+a_{1k}^{2}+a_{2k}^{2}+a_{3k}^{2}\Big)\frac{1}{2b_{3k}},\,\,(f_{k},f_{r})=0,\,\,k\not=r.

Set
a_{k}=(f_{k},f_{k})
and
b_{k}=M\xi_{k}
, then by Lemma
6.67
, (
6.3
) and (
5.22
) the proof is completed since

\hskip 51.21504pt\sum_{k}\frac{b_{k}^{2}}{a_{k}}=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{1}{2b_{3k}}\Big(\frac{1}{2b_{1k}}+\frac{1}{2b_{2k}}+\frac{1}{2b_{3k}}+a_{1k}^{2}+a_{2k}^{2}+a_{3k}^{2}\Big)^{-1}.\hskip 51.21504pt\Box

Now we would like to approximate
D_{3n}
by combinations of
\exp\big(is_{k}(x_{3k}-a_{3k}\big)iA_{kn}
. Set
s=(s_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}

\displaystyle\lambda_{k}^{(3)}(s_{k})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2b_{1k}}+\frac{1}{2b_{2k}}+\frac{1}{2b_{3k}}+a_{3k}^{2}-\Big(\frac{s^{2}}{4b^{2}_{3k}}+a_{3k}^{2}\Big)\exp\big(-\frac{s^{2}}{2b_{3k}}\big),

(5.24)

\displaystyle Y_{31}(s)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\frac{a_{1k}}{\sqrt{\lambda_{k}^{(3)}(s_{k})}}\right)_{k\in\mathbb{Z}},\hskip 9.24994ptY_{32}(s)=\left(\frac{a_{2k}}{\sqrt{\lambda_{k}^{(3)}(s_{k})}}\right)_{k\in\mathbb{Z}},

(5.25)

\displaystyle Y_{32}(s)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\Big(-\frac{s_{k}}{2b_{3k}}+ia_{3k}\Big)\exp\big(-\frac{s_{k}^{2}}{4b_{3k}}\big)}{\sqrt{\lambda_{k}^{(3)}(s_{k})}}\right)_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}.

In particular for
s_{k}=\sqrt{2b_{3k}}
we get

\lambda_{k}^{(3)}(s_{k})=\frac{1}{2b_{1k}}+\frac{1}{2b_{2k}}+\Big(\frac{1}{2b_{3k}}+a_{3k}^{2}\Big)\big(1-\frac{1}{e}\big).

(5.26)

Lemma 5.65
.

For any
n\in{\mathbb{Z}}
we have

D_{3n}{\bf 1}\in\langle\exp\big(is_{k}(x_{3k}-a_{3k}\big)iA_{kn}{\bf 1}\mid k\in{\mathbb{Z}}\rangle\,\,\Leftrightarrow\,\,\Delta(Y_{33}(s),Y_{31}(s),Y_{32}(s))=\infty.

Proof 35
.

We determine when the inclusion

\displaystyle D_{3n}{\bf 1}\in\langle\exp\big(is_{k}(x_{3k}-a_{3k}\big)iA_{kn}{\bf 1}=\Big(ix_{1k}\exp\big(is_{k}(x_{3k}-a_{3k})\big)D_{1n}

\displaystyle+ix_{2k}\exp\big(is_{k}(x_{3k}-a_{3k})\big)D_{2n}+ix_{3k}\exp\big(is_{k}(x_{3k}-a_{3k})\big)D_{3n}\Big){\bf 1}\mid k\in{\mathbb{Z}}\rangle

holds. Set
\xi_{rk}(s_{k})=ix_{rk}\exp\big(is_{k}(x_{3k}-a_{3k})\big)
for
1\leq r\leq 3
and

\displaystyle f_{k}(s_{k})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big(\xi_{1k}(s_{k})D_{1n}+\xi_{2k}(s_{k})D_{2n}+\big[\xi_{3k}(s_{k})-M\xi_{3k}(s_{k})\big]D_{3n}\Big){\bf 1}

(5.27)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big(ix_{1k}\exp\big(is_{k}(x_{3k}-a_{3k}\big)D_{1n}+ix_{2k}\exp\big(is_{k}(x_{3k}-a_{3k}\big)D_{2n}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\big[ix_{3k}\exp\big(is_{k}(x_{3k}-a_{3k}\big)-M\xi_{3k}(s_{k})\big]D_{3n}\Big){\bf 1}.

We show that

\displaystyle M\xi_{3k}(s)=\Big(-\frac{s}{2b_{3k}}+ia_{3k}\Big)\exp\Big(-\frac{s^{2}}{4b_{3k}}\Big),

(5.28)

\displaystyle(f_{k},f_{k})\sim\lambda_{k}^{(3)}(s_{k})+(g_{k},g_{k}),

(5.29)

\displaystyle(f_{k},f_{r})\sim a_{1k}a_{1r}+a_{2k}a_{2r}=(g_{k},g_{r}),

(5.30)

where
g_{k}=(a_{1k},a_{2k})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
. Indeed, set

F_{b}(s)=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\exp\big(is(x-a)\big)d\mu_{(b,a)}(x),

then

F_{b}(s)=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\exp\big(isx\big)d\mu_{(b,0)}(x)=\exp\big(-\frac{s^{2}}{4b}\Big),

(5.31)

where
d\mu_{(b,a)}(x)
and
d\mu_{(b,0)}(x)
are defined by (
5.23
).
Therefore,

\displaystyle H_{a,b}(s)=\int_{\mathbb{R}}ix\exp\big(is(x-a)\big)d\mu_{(b,a)}(x)=\int_{\mathbb{R}}i(x+a)\exp(isx)d\mu_{(b,0)}(x)

(5.32)

\displaystyle=\frac{dF_{b}(s)}{ds}+iaF_{b}(s)=\Big(-\frac{s}{2b}+ia\Big)\exp\Big(-\frac{s^{2}}{4b}\Big).

(5.33)

This implies (
5.28
). Further, to obtain (
5.29
) and (
5.30
) we write

\displaystyle(f_{k},f_{r})=\sum_{1\leq t,l\leq 2}(x_{tk},x_{lr})(D_{tn}{\bf 1},D_{ln}{\bf 1})=\sum_{1\leq t\leq 2}(x_{tk},x_{tr})(D_{tn}{\bf 1},D_{tn}{\bf 1})

\displaystyle=a_{1k}a_{1r}\frac{b_{1n}}{2}+a_{2k}a_{2r}\frac{b_{2n}}{2}\sim a_{1k}a_{1r}+a_{2k}a_{2r}=(g_{k},g_{r}),

\displaystyle(f_{k},f_{k})=\sum_{1\leq t\leq 2}\|x_{tk}\|^{2}\|D_{tn}{\bf 1}\|^{2}+\Big(M|\xi_{3k}(s_{k})|^{2}-|M\xi_{3k}(s_{k})|^{2}\Big)\|D_{3n}{\bf 1}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\Big(\frac{1}{2b_{1k}}+a_{1k}^{2}\Big)\frac{b_{1n}}{2}+\Big(\frac{1}{2b_{2k}}+a_{2k}^{2}\Big)\frac{b_{2n}}{2}+\Big(M|\xi_{3k}(s_{k})|^{2}-|M\xi_{3k}(s_{k})|^{2}\Big)\frac{b_{3n}}{2}

\displaystyle\sim\Big(\frac{1}{2b_{1k}}+a_{1k}^{2}\Big)+\Big(\frac{1}{2b_{2k}}+a_{2k}^{2}\Big)+\Big(M|\xi_{3k}(s_{k})|^{2}-|M\xi_{3k}(s_{k})|^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle=\lambda_{k}^{(3)}(s_{k})+(g_{k},g_{k}).

Set for brevity
\xi(s)=ix\exp\big(is(x-a)\big)
, then

\displaystyle f(s):=M|\xi(s)|^{2}-|M\xi(s)|^{2}=\Big(\frac{1}{2b}+a^{2}\Big)-\Big(\frac{s^{2}}{4b^{2}}+a^{2}\Big)\exp\Big(-\frac{s^{2}}{2b}\Big),

\displaystyle\min_{s}f(s)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\frac{1-e^{-(1-2ba^{2})}}{2b}+a^{2},&\,\text{if}\,\,1-2ba^{2}\geq 0\\
\frac{1}{2b},&\,\text{if}\,\,1-2ba^{2}<0\end{array}\right..

Indeed, consider the function

g_{a,b}(x)=\Big(\frac{x^{2}}{2b}+a^{2}\Big)\exp(-x^{2})
and set
x_{0}=\sqrt{1-2ba^{2}}
.
We have

\max_{x\in\mathbb{R}}g_{a,b}(x)\!=\!\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}g_{a,b}(x_{0})=\frac{\exp(-(1-2ba^{2}))}{2b},&\text{if}\,\,1-2ba^{2}\geq 0,\\
g_{a,b}(0)=a^{2},&\text{if}\,\,1-2ba^{2}<0.\end{array}\right.

(5.37)

To calculate
\lambda_{k}^{(3)}(s_{k})
, we get finally

\displaystyle\lambda_{k}^{(3)}(s_{k})\!=\!\frac{1}{2b_{1k}}\!+\!a_{1k}^{2}\!+\!\frac{1}{2b_{2k}}\!+\!a_{2k}^{2}\!+\!\Big(M|\xi_{3k}(s_{k})|^{2}\!-\!|M\xi_{3k}(s_{k})|^{2}\Big)\!-\!(g_{k},g_{k})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2b_{1k}}+\frac{1}{2b_{2k}}+\frac{1}{2b_{3k}}+a_{1k}^{2}+a_{2k}^{2}+a_{3k}^{2}-\Big(\frac{s_{k}^{2}}{4b^{2}_{3k}}+a_{3k}^{2}\Big)\exp\big(-\frac{s_{k}^{2}}{2b_{3k}}\big)

\displaystyle-(g_{k},g_{k})=\frac{1}{2b_{1k}}+\frac{1}{2b_{2k}}+\frac{1}{2b_{3k}}+a_{3k}^{2}-\Big(\frac{s_{k}^{2}}{4b^{2}_{3k}}+a_{3k}^{2}\Big)\exp\big(-\frac{s_{k}^{2}}{2b_{3k}}\big)=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2b_{1k}}+\frac{1}{2b_{2k}}+\Big(\frac{1}{2b_{3k}}+a_{3k}^{2}\Big)\big(1-e^{-1}\big),\quad\text{for}\quad\frac{s_{k}^{2}}{2b_{3k}}=1.

Therefore, we get (
5.24
) and (
5.26
)

\lambda_{k}^{(3)}(s_{k})=\frac{1}{2b_{1k}}+\frac{1}{2b_{2k}}+\Big(\frac{1}{2b_{3k}}+a_{3k}^{2}\Big)\big(1-\frac{1}{e}\big).

For
A_{2m+1}=\big((f_{k}(s_{k}),f_{r}(s_{r}))\big)_{k,r=-m}^{m},
and

b=(M\xi_{3k}(s_{k}))_{k=-m}^{m}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2m+1}
we have

A_{2m+1}=\sum_{k=-m}^{m}\lambda_{k}E_{kk}+\gamma(g_{-m},\dots,g_{0},\dots,g_{m}).

The proof is now finished on invoking Lemma
7.72
.
